# P5: one MAWS across machines and people (the desk folded in) (2026-10-27)

> **Parent:** `docs/charter.md` (root), as its phase P5. **Level name:** P5. This document is doperpowers:brainstorming's design (owner rounds of 2026-10-26) extended in place by doperpowers:decomposing: design up top, roadmap below. Children dispatch per their grain hint, each carrying its section as its spec; each child's artifact opens by citing this document (path + child id + parent pin). Child ids are `O1`–`O17`; contracts are the remote layer's `RL1`–`RL10`, lifted below, and this document's own `OX1`–`OX7`.
>
> **Cites, as binding where it says so:** the remote layer, `docs/doperpowers/specs/2026-10-26-remote-layer-design.md` (PR #183 at 65d7fa11e, remote-architecture; the one trust model, cited as *RL §n*); the desk design, `docs/doperpowers/specs/2026-10-15-desk-design.md` (*desk §n*), which this document supersedes where it says so; the desk control surface, `docs/doperpowers/specs/2026-10-22-desk-control-surface-design.md` (MW1–MW5, merged); the E10 supervisor, `docs/doperpowers/plans/2026-10-06-e10-supervisor.md`. **Research behind it** (`research/org/`): the headless-cut survey at master c3507722d (*survey §n*), the identity prior art (*identity §n*), and the remote research and holder design in `research/desk-remote/` (PR #79).
>
> **Writers.** The MAWS architect writes this document. desk-sminos-orchestrator co-designed it and owns the desk-side children (O10–O14); remote-architecture owns the remote layer document. A desk-side child's residue lands in this Decision Log under its id, written by this document's writer.

## Purpose

**Higher purpose.** The charter's: a general, state-of-the-art workstation for supervising AI coding agents on the Claude Code engine, for its owner and a few colleagues, with supervision of many sessions and depth in one weighing equally (charter §1). The owner's work is not on one machine. It lives in projects whose sessions run on a MacBook, a Mac mini and Linux hosts, and the desk (desk §Purpose) was built to reach them from a browser. Two products grew toward the same job from two ends: MAWS with the depth, the desk with the reach. This phase makes them one.

**What it is for.** One MAWS app, signed in as one person of an org, shows every machine that person may see, the org's projects, and the sessions in each, and drives each session at MAWS's own depth on whatever machine it runs. A session opened from MAWS has the full SDK surface anywhere (lane B). A session started in a terminal is controlled through the bridge mod, with the gaps shown honestly (lane A), on its own machine as much as on another. People cooperate mainly through their own agents, which message each other with `sminos say` as today, each session labelled with who owns it (owner, 2026-10-26).

### The scene at its best

The owner opens MAWS on the MacBook. The sidebar lists projects, not folders: `MAWS` holds `maws-fe @macbook`, `maws-45 @mini` and `ci-opt @dc-1`, each row with its machine. `maws-45` was opened from MAWS, so it runs under the mini's machine daemon. Its transcript streams as if it were local. Its permission card shows the exact command. The image dropped on the composer reaches the model on the mini. The diff, the file tab and the terminal in the panel are the mini's worktree. `maws-fe` was started in a terminal on the MacBook. It is no longer read-only: send, steer, interrupt, answer and the model menu work through the bridge, and what the mod can't reach is greyed with its reason.

"+ Session" asks for a machine as well as a project. The lid closes. `maws-45` keeps working on the mini with every MAWS tool, and its next question buzzes the phone. The answer from the phone moves it on. When the MacBook wakes, every stream resumes from its cursor within a second. Opening MAWS on the mini itself shows the same sessions in the same state. Later a colleague joins the org. They see the projects' listings and their own sessions. Their agent asks `maws-fe` for a column name with `sminos say`, and the message arrives marked as coming from their agent, owned by them.

### What it must not be

- **Slower or weaker locally.** MAWS with no sign-in works as today, and a local session's events reach the screen within OX7's budget of today's path. The hub is never on the local path.
- **A thin remote viewer.** A session on a machine with no app keeps its MAWS tools, derivations and approvals. The machine is the authority (RL §1).
- **A second store of transcripts.** They stay on the machine that ran them (charter D-12; desk §What it must not be).
- **One key for everyone, or a hub that can act.** Every action and event names its writer, and the machine checks the writer (RL §5).
- **A patched engine** (charter D-1).
- **A web edition first.** The web port waits until this matures (owner, 2026-10-26). Client classes keep its place (RL §5.10).

## Parent-level acceptance

P5 closes when all of the following hold on the owner's MacBook, the mini, one devcloud Linux VM and the phone, checked by hand or by the named gate and recorded with evidence in Outcomes.

1. **Local parity.** Signed out, MAWS on the MacBook does everything it did at master c3507722d, with every e2e group green on the daemon shape, and the local-latency bench (OX7) within budget. Every project, session, artifact, library item, draft and layout from before the upgrade is present after it, with no re-index.
2. **Sessions outlive the app with their services.** With the app quit, a lane B session's `maws` tool calls (an artifact write, a dataset refresh) complete, the index and attention keep deriving, and reopening shows no visible gap: the journal re-serves what the ring dropped.
3. **One sidebar across machines.** Signed in, the sidebar groups sessions from the three machines by org project, each row with its machine. States match `sminos list --org` within 2 s. Sessions in no project sit under their machine.
4. **Lane B at full depth anywhere.** From the MacBook, a lane B session on the mini and one on the Linux VM each: stream with token deltas, send, steer, interrupt, approve and deny, answer a question, take an image, show the diff, open and edit a file, run a terminal in the worktree, and open, edit and screenshot an artifact. On the bench, remote delta-to-paint p50 is at most the local p50 plus one round trip plus 10 ms, a command's round trip is at most two round trips plus 20 ms, open to first row is ≤ 1.5 s, and resume is ≤ engine start plus one round trip, on each transport the app uses (§5).
5. **Spawn anywhere.** "+ Session" on the mini or the VM starts a lane B session that is in the sidebar within 5 s of the engine's first event.
6. **Lane A in MAWS.** A terminal session on the MacBook and one on the mini can be sent to, steered, interrupted, answered and approved from MAWS, and their model and effort set. Every control the bridge can't reach is disabled with its reason. A terminal session without the bridge stays read-only, as today.
7. **The laptop asleep.** With the MacBook asleep for an hour, sessions on the mini and the VM keep working, their approvals wait, and a question and a permission prompt each reach the phone and are answered there. Within 1 s of the network being reachable after wake, every subscription resumes from its cursor, with no loss and no duplicate (RL3).
8. **Two clients.** MAWS on the mini and on the MacBook attach to one session at once. Both stream it, and an answer from one settles the card in the other.
9. **The machine decides.** The trust cases of RL's acceptance hold: hub-placed actions and events are refused, an altered request card is refused by digest, and a removed device is refused once the new org state arrives.
10. **Agent tools on a machine with no app.** On the Linux VM with no app attached, `artifact_screenshot` and `artifact_export` work through the machine's render service, and browser control is offered or refused by capability, never failed mid-call.
11. **Owners are visible and carried.** Every row of a session you don't own alone shows its owners (Decision Log 111). A `sminos say` across sessions carries the sender session's owners, signed by its machine (RL9), and the receiver draws it as a peer message from that agent and owner.
12. **The people model is in.** A second member, added by an org-state edit on the staging org, sees listings per each session's visibility and their own sessions in full; with sessions at the default `listed`, `converse` and `approve` are refused on sessions not shared with them.
13. **Upgrades mid-turn.** With a lane B session mid-turn and running a background shell: `mawsd` restarted, and the app upgraded one minor, leaves the turn completing, no input applied twice and the pending permission shown once; after O16 the same holds for an engine-host upgrade.
14. **The laptop's cost.** With the app closed and no session working, `mawsd` and the engine host on the MacBook stay under an idle CPU and memory bound set by O2's measurement of today's supervisor, memory read as the two processes' total physical footprint (Decision Logs 48 and 57), and idle sessions are released by the laptop's policy (§2). On a machine enrolled in the org, the endpoint `mawsd` then loads has a budget of its own beside that bound: 8 MiB of physical footprint, and an idle CPU share set from its enrolled reading on the mini (Decision Log 114).
15. **The local boundary.** Every local socket is owner-checked and refuses symlinks, authenticates before any session data (RL §5.11; a refusal line naming its protocol, build and live count, readable by the same uid only, is allowed, Decision Log 29), and the daemon's artifact endpoint is reachable only through a unix socket or a per-boot token, never an open loopback port.
16. **The owner's verdict.** After a week of daily use across the machines, the owner declares MAWS the one interface, and says what still sent them to a terminal or to desk-web.

## Design

### 1. The shape

```
  member's Mac: MAWS app                            hub (Worker + DOs)              any machine (one OS account)
  ┌───────────────────────────────┐                 ┌────────────────────┐          ┌───────────────────────────────────────┐
  │ renderer (no keys, no paths   │                 │ Registry: org state│   wss    │ mawsd, the machine daemon (Node)      │
  │  of its own; machine-scoped)  │  wss, sealed    │ Machine DO: relay  │ ◀──────▶ │  endpoint module: keys, policy, RL1–4 │
  │ main: windows, client port,   │ ◀─────────────▶ │ Project DO: log    │          │  services: index, read model, sessions│
  │  device keys, connections     │                 └────────────────────┘          │   attention, owner channel, git, scm, │
  └──────────────┬────────────────┘                                                 │   files, library, connectors, catalog │
                 │ same-uid socket (the app's own machine, no hub, no sign-in)      │  ├─ engine host (E10 supervisor):     │
                 └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────▶ │  │   Query per session, session hosts │
                                                                                    │  │   └ claude -p (lane B)              │
                                                                                    │  ├─ pty host                          │
                                                                                    │  └─ render service (Chromium; U4)     │
                                                                                    └──────────────▲────────────────────────┘
                                                                                     RL10, local   │
                                                                                    ┌──────────────┴────────────────────────┐
                                                                                    │ sminos daemon (Python, its own unit): │
                                                                                    │  log, delivery, lane A plane          │
                                                                                    │   └ bridge mod in terminal claude     │
                                                                                    └───────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**[binding — the owner's direction (2026-10-26) and the survey's sizing.]** Every machine runs one machine daemon, `mawsd`. It holds the endpoint (the machine's only hub connection, its key, its trust boundary and policy: RL §2), as a module, and MAWS's machine-bound services, which leave Electron main. The engine host stays a separate process under it, so restarting or upgrading `mawsd` never ends a session (Desktop's helper lesson, RL §2.1); once O16's holder lands, an engine-host restart re-adopts its sessions too. The Electron app becomes a client of daemons: its own machine's over a same-uid socket, others' through RL2 channels. The Query of a lane B session stays on the session's machine (owner, 2026-10-26). The alternative, Desktop's SSH split with the Query in the app, costs about the same code (survey §6: 10k–16k new and changed lines against 11k–20k) and leaves a second client, a sleeping laptop and an app-less Linux VM unsolved.

### 2. The machine

**Processes.**
- **`mawsd`** is plain Node, run by a LaunchAgent on macOS and a systemd user unit on Linux. It is the services host of survey §3: main's moved services under a second composition root, built from the same injected dependencies. It is the engine host's single client, as main is today, so E10's single-client machinery (the connect burst, the undelivered tail, `ready`) stays correct; the multi-client fan-out with a cursor per stream and client (RL3) is new code in `mawsd`. The endpoint is a narrow module with its own package and tests; a lint rule lets only it import the key and policy code (architect's ruling, RL Decision Log 6).
- **The engine host** is E10's detached supervisor. It holds the Query of each lane B session (its `Session`), the `maws` tool server and the hook callbacks (survey §6; RL at 65d7fa11e). Restarting `mawsd` drops its client connection, not its children. Through most of P5 it keeps spawning `claude` directly, so an engine-host crash, or an upgrade across an OX1 major, ends its lane B sessions, which reopen with `--resume` (today's behaviour); within a major, an old engine host keeps serving its sessions until it is idle and is then replaced. O16 hardens it as the holder (holder design §3.1–§3.3): a session host per process, the seq ring, the stdin offset, the writer fence and re-adoption, so an engine-host upgrade on a server that never idles ends nothing; from its M3, an upgrade across an OX1 major no longer ends lane B sessions either, since a capable engine host asked to exit by a client that adopts hands its sessions over (Decision Log 167).
- **The pty host** is forked by `mawsd`, so terminals stop dying with the app (survey §3 S2), with a binary `pty.data` frame.
- **The render service** is Chromium owned by the daemon, for offscreen capture, export and the artifact scheme of agent tools (§6). Two shapes are open and U4's spike decides between them: a second, windowless instance of the app binary beside a plain-Node `mawsd` (headless Chromium on Linux), or `mawsd` itself running as windowless Electron (Electron's headless platform on Linux), which moves capture and export verbatim, keeps `safeStorage` continuity (U7 disappears) and drops a render protocol, at about 150–200 MB more resident memory. The criterion: the single-process shape wins if it runs reliably under a LaunchAgent and on a devcloud VM and its idle memory fits acceptance 14.
- **The sminos daemon** stays its own Python process and unit, and becomes the endpoint module's local client: its log, delivery and lane A plane stay; its uplink, enrollment, HMAC coding and policy move to the endpoint (RL §2.1, RL10).

**Why a services host and not a merge into the supervisor.** The index is synchronous `node:sqlite`, and the library and full-text fill would share one loop with event batching, the `maws` tool server and Yjs commits (survey §3). A merge stays possible later.

**Two composition roots until M2 closes.** The moved services stay a library with two roots: `mawsd`'s and main's embedded one (they already take injected `register`, `emit` and `call`; `src/main/index.ts:226-760`). A profile setting flips between `embedded` and `daemon`; the component harness runs embedded; if a landing step breaks OX7 or daily use, the owner's profile flips back instead of living with it. The embedded root is deleted at O15.

**One daemon per profile.** Today a supervisor is per profile (`src/engine-host/supervisor-files.ts:216-219`), and the e2e harness gives every app a fresh profile (`e2e/support/launch.ts:116`). So the LaunchAgent or systemd unit serves the default profile, and every other profile (an `electron-vite dev` run, an e2e run) gets a private, app-started daemon that exits when idle, which is E10's mechanism generalised. Tests never touch the owner's daemon. A second profile's daemon, with its own `MachineRef`, is also how one Mac stands in for two machines in tests (O6).

**`mawsd` has a command line**, the only window into a machine with no app: `status`, `restart`, `logs`, `enroll`, `keys`, and applying a pending revocation (RL §5.2).

**Settings split three ways.** One `settings.json` today mixes fields of three owners (survey §2 item 12, §7.7). The client's (theme, notifications, layout, window state) stay with the app. The machine's (engine, launch profile, supervisor, MCP servers, sessions) move to `mawsd`. The person's (Discover's catalog sources, Send-to destinations, connector accounts) belong to the signed-in person and live with the client device until they move into the person's signed member entry (RL5), so two Macs agree; machine-side credentials, like an MCP server's OAuth, sign in per machine, with consent opened on the client and the callback relayed to the machine (survey §2 item 7).

**Idle release.** Interest is the union of every client's open tabs and subscriptions, reported to the daemon (OX5), replacing the sweep's read of `layout.json` (survey §7.6). Release follows the machine's role: a laptop keeps today's rule, released 10 minutes after the last interest, because 20 idle sessions are several GB the owner gets back; a server (the mini, a VM) releases after 24 hours by wall clock with no interest and no turn, since today's rule would stop every session on a machine no app attaches to (survey §7.9). O16 replaces the server rule with the holder's verified-empty rule (owner F2, 2026-10-22), and the laptop's release arms the same fence, since a turn lost in the window is lost on either role (Decision Log 167). A session can be marked keep. Release is strict on every machine (owner, 2026-10-27): a session holding any background work is never released, whatever its clock reads. That means a background Bash job, a running subagent, a Monitor, a session cron, an MCP task or a workflow. Ambient tasks, which the engine marks as its own housekeeping (every `skip_transcript` task, such as a memory extraction, and auto-started watchers), are not background work: E10's rule stands (`src/engine-host/budget.ts:40-48`), since a watcher would otherwise hold its process forever. Tasks are read from `background_tasks_changed`, a level signal that stays known across interrupts. Crons have no stream signal, so the engine host reads Stop's `session_crons` through a new Stop hook callback. Durable crons appear in no Stop, so it also reads `<cwd>/.claude/scheduled_tasks.json`, which a durable `CronCreate` writes, at each Stop and at a resume's launch. It counts the entries whose `createdBySessionId` is any engine session id the session has had (Decision Log 46). A work state the daemon can't read counts as work, and that state is narrow: crons after a cron tool ran in a turn that ended without Stop (an interrupt, an API error), until the next Stop; a resume that doesn't follow a release, until its first Stop, since `--resume` resurrects the session-only crons and one can fire before that Stop (Decision Log 47), unless the engine host's state file shows that the session's last Stop listed no session cron and no cron tool ran after it (Decision Log 49); a scheduled-tasks file that exists but can't be read; and a re-adopted process until a task snapshot or change arrives, which from O16's M3 narrows to a re-adoption whose journal has a gap, since the journal and an exact replay are the daemon reading that state (Decision Log 167). O2's probe found no keepalive in `-p`, so Decision Log 29's conditional fourth state does not exist.

**Install, versions and runtime.** `mawsd`, the engine host, the pty host and the endpoint ship as one headless package, versioned apart from the app, with its own Node runtime on Linux; on a Mac with the app the unit may run the app's binary as Node (survey §7.4). Every hello carries a protocol major and minor and capabilities (OX1), never an exact build match. Until signed builds exist (owner, 2026-10-26: an Apple Developer ID is coming), machines install from a source checkout, and device and machine keys are software keys (RL §5.1). The package's decisions are O2's: the Linux runtime and `node:sqlite`'s Node floor, node-pty's Linux build, the uid as the boundary where the darwin-only peer-auth addon is absent, the devenv step for a devcloud VM, the unit's program path when the owner runs from a checkout, and the login-shell capture under a unit that has no login shell. The org-project rule (§3) is sminos's; `mawsd` implements it against a conformance fixture shared by both repositories, with sminos's as the reference.

**Inference per machine.** A machine's sessions use that machine's Claude login or its gateway (charter D-19's relay where ClaudeUsage runs, else the machine's own login), recorded in the session record as its inference route (RL6), never as a token. Account-managed inference through a server-side gateway is a later phase (owner, 2026-10-26).

### 3. The app

The renderer keeps almost all of its code; it never held Electron or Node (research §0.6). Main shrinks to the client: windows, the client port, device keys, the connection manager, voice, and the in-app browser for the person's own browsing.

- **The client port (OX4).** Every effect that only a client can perform (pick and save dialogs, reveal, open-external, notifications, dock and badge, focus) is one injected port that the daemon calls through the client that asked. The daemon never imports Electron.
- **The connection manager.** One connection per machine in use: the local daemon's socket, and an RL2 channel per remote machine. RL2 runs over either carrier: a direct socket to the endpoint on the tailnet, tried first with a short timeout, or the hub's relay (RL1's multiplex). Every machine in the acceptance set is on the owner's tailnet, so remote depth runs at network latency rather than an edge round trip, and survives a hub outage; the hub stays for discovery, presence, listings, the log plane, the phone and machines off the tailnet. O11's U2 measurement sets the default. A machine reachable only directly is drawn as hub-offline, channel-up. A machine's departure greys that machine's sessions and nothing else (survey §7.11).
- **Machine-scoped addresses (OX2).** Every machine-bound id is addressed `machine/id`, where `machine` is a stable id minted once per profile at its first run, by whichever of the app and `mawsd` runs first, into `<profile>/machine.json` (Decision Log 27), and never changes, independent of the machine key, which a rotation can replace, so a signed-out Mac has one and neither a rename, an enrollment nor a key change alters it; the org state's machine entry carries the ref beside the key (RL5's `ref`, `[A-Za-z0-9_-]{16,64}`), and the enrolled name is shown from the org state. Renderer stores key by address; the layout's keys carry the machine. Ids are addresses in the renderer: its client lifts every branded id it receives to `machine/id` and lowers it on send, so the 218 call sites that carry an id route by the address they hold, and the 75 that carry only a path take an explicit machine (survey §4.4; Decision Log 21).
- **Org projects (OX3).** A project is the org's project, as sminos derives it (`.smns`, else the repository's remote on an enrolled machine, else the owning repository), not a local folder. Each machine contributes checkouts of it. The same repository on the MacBook and the mini is one project with two checkouts, and sessions with no project sit under their machine's own project (owner, 2026-10-26; survey §7.10).

### 4. Sessions in MAWS: the two lanes

**[binding — owner decisions of 2026-10-19, 10-22 and 10-24, kept.]** The lane follows where a session started. A session opened from MAWS is lane B on any machine whose daemon runs; a session started in a terminal or as an engine `--bg` job is lane A, controlled by the bridge mod and never moved live (F7). The app draws controls from each session's capability list (RL7, from lane-b-control-map's shared shapes), never from the lane name.

- **Lane B** is MAWS as it is today, served from the session's machine: every control request, every dialog, the `maws` tool server and hooks, rewind, media.
- **Lane A** replaces the read-only foreign-live pane (charter D-12, amended here). The bridge reaches send, steer, interrupt, answers, approvals, model and effort, slash commands, side questions, compact, MCP toggles and media through MW1–MW5. Approvals race the terminal's own dialog and the card says so. A terminal session without the bridge mod stays read-only, with "Take over here" as today. After a lane A process ends, its conversation reopens as lane B with `--resume`.
- **Local lane A needs no sign-in.** The app reaches the bridge through its machine's services, over the bridge's own socket; an endpoint serves RL10 only while its machine is org-listed (Decision Logs 55 and 93).

### 5. Remote surfaces

Everything the panel shows for a local session works for a remote one, over the session's channel.
- **Diff, files and the file editor** read and write through the machine's fs service with ranged, inode-checked reads.
- **The terminal panel** is a shell in the remote worktree from that machine's pty host, with offset de-duplication on the channel (RL §4.8). It is machine-level execution, so it needs the machine owners' standing or an explicit shell right (OX9). This is MAWS's own terminal, not a terminal into a Claude Code session, which F5 (2026-10-23) still rules out.
- **Artifacts and the library** are served over the daemon's HTTP artifact endpoint, which the local app reaches directly and a remote app through the channel; edits travel as Yjs updates as today.
- **Drag and drop** uploads bytes to the session's machine (into its attachments directory) before the message is sent; paths in a message are the machine's.
- **The browser for the person.** A remote dev server is reached by forwarding its port through the channel to the app's in-app browser. The agent's own browser on a remote session runs on the session's machine and is seen from a remote app by screencast (U3, settled by O5 M7; built by O17).

### 6. Agent tools bound to Chromium

Some `maws` tools need Chromium: `artifact_screenshot`, `artifact_export`, Send to's PDF and DOCX, and browser control (survey §5). On a machine with no app these are agent capabilities lost, not views. So they move onto the machine:
- **Capture and export** go to the render service, which answers with files on the machine or bytes. The attached client is the wrong home, since its fonts and its copy of the artifact are not the machine's.
- **The artifact scheme** becomes an HTTP endpoint on the daemon, keeping today's ranges, sandbox CSP and separate origin; the local app's `protocol.handle` proxies to it.
- **Browser control.** The daemon owns a stable per-session cua socket, checked against the engine pid it holds, so a session launched with no app still gets a browser for that launch (survey §7.5). U3 settled it (O5 M7, Decision Log 186): the tab engine runs on the session's machine, the app if it is attached there, else the render host in offscreen tab views, and a remote app sees it by screencast, because the agent's tab must reach the machine's localhost; O17 builds it.
- **Pane display** (`show_pane`) stays client-bound: it opens in the app of the device that sent the turn's input, else the most recently focused attached app. With notifications, it is the only client-bound tool; this narrows RL §3.1's list, and the divergence is recorded there.

### 7. People

The org is the owner alone for the next months, and the people model is built in from the start, so a second member is an org-state edit, not a redesign (owner, 2026-10-26).

- **Owners.** A session belongs to the person who spawned it: a terminal session to its machine's owner, a MAWS spawn to the signed requester, a `sminos spawn` to the spawning session's owners, copied at spawn (RL §5.8). Owners can add owners ("A, B and 43+").
- **Visibility** is set per session by its owners, like a shared document: `private`, `listed` (the default: every member sees owner, machine and state, contents locked), `org-view` or `org-converse`. Shares grant `view`, `converse` or `approve` (owner, 2026-10-26; RL §6.2).
- **Machines** have owners (members or the org) and per-machine rights per member; spawning on someone else's machine needs its owners' grant (owner, 2026-10-26; RL §6.3).
- **Agents talk as today.** A message between agents of different people is today's `sminos say`, plus the owner label of the sending session, signed by its machine (RL9). It never counts as a person's words and never approves anything, which is today's engine rule as well (identity §2). No new delivery policy (owner, 2026-10-26).
- **Credentials.** The session record names its principal and its inference route. Who may drive a session on someone else's personal login is decided by the later account-managed inference phase, not built here (owner, 2026-10-26, after the terms finding of identity §3).

### 8. The interface

- **The sidebar** keeps Codex's flat project tree (charter D-2) with org projects. Each project lists the person's own sessions; a per-project toggle adds other people's, each with its owner and machine (owner, 2026-10-26). Rows carry a machine chip; an offline machine's rows grey with "machine offline".
- **The project channel.** Each project gets a channel view, opened like a tab: the desk's group chat (desk §The scene) in MAWS. It shows everything the project's sessions sent to a person (to-human messages, questions, permission cards) across machines, agent-to-agent traffic folded to one row, and people's own messages. The cards in it are the same pending requests attention shows, so answering in either place settles both; the channel adds no second state. To-human messages and asks travel on each machine's channels, not the hub's log (RL §6.2), so the channel assembles them per machine, and an offline machine's rows show from the app's cache. **[review: taste]**
- **"+ Session"** gains a machine picker (default: this Mac), the checkout and worktree of the project on that machine, and says which lane and which inference route the session will get.
- **The Org page** in Settings: sign-in, members and their devices, machines with enrollment codes, per-machine rights, and the recovery key's id with the root-transition flow (the recovery key itself is offline and fixed at genesis, RL §5.3; Decision Log 98). Desk-web's org, key and machines views (`apps/desk-web/src/views/{org,key,machines}`) are its reference.
- **Overview and attention** span machines; the dock badge and ⌘⇧J count every reachable session.

### 9. The phone

Desk Web pauses, and its phone loop stays: push notifications, and question and permission cards, and nothing else (owner, 2026-10-26). It moves to the `web-cards` client class over RL2 in WebCrypto with a per-action WebAuthn assertion (RL §5.10). Desk-web's other views freeze and stop being maintained. A MAWS web port is a later phase.

### 10. Local parity and the latency budget

Local is where MAWS spends most of its time, and the new shape adds hops: today main talks to the engine host; afterwards the app talks to `mawsd`, which talks to the engine host, and after O16 a session host sits between the engine host and `claude`. **OX7** binds a bench, built in O1 and run on the gate's Mac lane, that measures a lane B session's delta-to-paint (p50 and p95) and command round trip on the local path. Each landing step of the cut, O16 included, stays within 2 ms p50 and 5 ms p95 of the c3507722d baseline, or the owner's profile flips back to embedded and the hop is redesigned. The remote numbers of acceptance 4 run on the same bench.

### 11. Cutover

Per machine, with single-trust inbound (RL §9; outbound events are dual-coded until the org key retires): each machine upgrades to `mawsd`, re-enrolls in place under its name, re-attests its own log history once, and from then takes no DX6. A devcloud acceptance VM cuts over first and proves RL1–RL4. `web-cards` ships no later than the cutover of the first machine whose sessions the owner answers from the phone. The mini's sminos upgrade (mini-ops) is a prerequisite of its cutover. The shared org key is retired when the last machine and the phone have moved.

### 12. What this changes in the charter

Recorded in charter §19 on approval:
- **§1 Purpose** gains the org: machines and people, with the desk folded in; the desk's "not MAWS's web edition" (desk §1, 2026-10-15) is retired.
- **D-12** is amended: a live terminal session with the bridge mod is controlled through it (lane A); read-only "foreign-live" remains for sessions without it.
- **D-16** is superseded: sessions and MAWS's services outlive the app under `mawsd`.
- **D-11** is narrowed: the app stays macOS arm64; `mawsd` runs on macOS and Linux.
- **D-19** is amended: the inference route is per machine.
- **§4.1 Process model** is replaced by §1–§3 here.

## Silent decisions

- `mawsd` is the name of the machine daemon and its package.
- The bench of OX7 lives beside the existing tool-time guard of P4 and runs on the gate's Mac lane.
- `ProjectId` stays a UUID; an org project is a separate id with a checkout list, so existing local data migrates by attaching each local project to its org project.
- The browser's `browser_*` tables leave `index.sqlite` for the client (survey §2 item 1).
- The quit guard shrinks to unsaved files (survey §2 item 13).
- Desk-web's tickets #177–#179 (web panels) close; #180 (whole-PDF spike) stays.

## Children

The cut follows the survey's sequence (S0–S7) on the MAWS side and the remote layer's interfaces on the desk side. Two tracks run in parallel and meet at O7: the network and lane A (O1, O2, O6, O9, O10–O12) give the owner every session on every machine in one sidebar first; the daemon cut (O3–O5) then brings lane B's full depth to every machine. Each child's executor is a fresh context; the grain hint names the brief's grain.

### O1: Wire compatibility, seams and the bench — plan

**Purpose.** Make main's services movable without moving them: the prerequisite of every later step (survey §3 S0).
**Acceptance.** `MainContext` carries a `ClientRef` instead of a `WebContents` in all 34 files; Electron-only effects go through the client port (OX4); a registry owner value forwards a namespace to another process transparently; the exact wire fingerprint is replaced by OX1's compatibility range, proved by a test that runs an app build against a daemon build one minor apart; every namespace in the registry declares its grade (OX9), and a test fails the build on one that doesn't; the OX7 bench runs on the gate and records the c3507722d baseline. The app's behaviour is unchanged.
**Edges.** Blocks O2, O3, O6. Blocked by nothing.
**Contracts.** OX1, OX4, OX7, OX9.
**Inheritance.** Survey §3 S0 and §7.1 [advisory].

### O2: `mawsd` lifecycle, packaging and the pty host — plan

**Purpose.** Give every machine its daemon process: the home of the endpoint module (O10) and, later, the services (survey §3 S1–S2).
**Acceptance.**
1. A LaunchAgent (macOS) or systemd user unit (Linux) runs `mawsd` for the default profile, and `mawsd` owns the engine host; the app connects to it or starts it. Every other profile (dev, e2e) gets a private app-started daemon that exits when idle (§2).
2. The pty host is forked by `mawsd`, and a terminal survives an app quit.
3. Idle release follows §2's per-role rule on interest (OX5), with a keep mark, and is strict. A test per kind shows that a session holding a background Bash job, a subagent, a Monitor, a session cron, an MCP task or a workflow is never released, that an ambient task alone doesn't hold it, and that each of §2's unreadable states blocks release. Crons come from Stop's `session_crons` through a Stop hook callback in the engine host, which nothing reads today, and durable crons from the checkout's `.claude/scheduled_tasks.json`, read at each Stop and at a resume's launch (Decision Log 46).
4. The supervisor e2e suites run on the daemon shape.
5. `mawsd`'s command line (`status`, `restart`, `logs`, `enroll`, `keys`) works on a machine with no app.
6. The package installs on a devcloud VM with no app, through a devenv step, and stays up across logout with lingering (U8). Its named decisions are recorded: the Linux runtime and Node floor, node-pty's Linux build, the uid boundary without peer auth, the unit's program path from a checkout, and login-shell capture under a unit.
7. The idle CPU and physical footprint of today's supervisor are measured, and acceptance 14's bound is set from them; the bare runtime's footprint is recorded beside them as a diagnostic, and `mawsd` alone is measured at its first build as an early check against the bound (Decision Logs 48 and 57).
**Edges.** Blocked by O1. Blocks O3, O10.
**Contracts.** OX1, OX5.
**Required.** For parent acceptance.
**Status.** document landed (`docs/doperpowers/plans/2026-10-27-p5-o2-mawsd.md`, master d35bfd612); landed (PR #296, master 7a5893b2c, Decision Log 107).

### O10: The endpoint module and the protocol package (desk side) — plan

**Purpose.** It gives every machine one authenticated, encrypted way in. The endpoint module inside `mawsd`:
- terminates the hub socket (RL1), device channels (RL2), subscriptions (RL3) and discrete actions (RL4);
- enforces the machine's policy and each session's grades;
- hands lane A work to sminos over RL10.

One protocol package holds both halves, the endpoint's and the client's, with shared test vectors. The app's main and desk-web's WebCrypto build (O13) import it, so each end of a channel runs the same code.

**Acceptance.**
1. **One implementation.** The test vectors for RL2's handshake and frames, RL4's canonical action and digest, and RL8's event signature pass in both halves, under Node crypto and WebCrypto.
2. **Resume without loss.** Against O11's staging hub, a device opens a channel to a machine and subscribes to a lane A and a lane B session. It resumes by cursor, with no loss and no duplicate, across:
   - a dropped hub socket;
   - a hub deploy (stale-generation);
   - a `mawsd` restart.
3. **The trust cases.** Each of these holds on the staging hub:
   - an action replayed by the relay is refused;
   - an action past 60 s is refused;
   - a reseal with the same opId returns the stored result and runs nothing;
   - a lost acknowledgement resolves through `query {opId, opCreated}`, and an op older than RL4's `OP_HORIZON` is refused `expired`, so a pruned ledger never reopens an id;
   - a frame altered in the relay closes the channel;
   - a device removed by a new org state is refused at once, and so is one removed by a direct revocation. The machine acknowledges only after the removal is durable.
4. **Policy moves intact.** Today's `sminos_policy` behaviour holds, now at the endpoint:
   - read-only mode refuses every action;
   - a spawn above the ceiling is refused;
   - a spawn into an unknown checkout is refused;
   - a local `sminos spawn` stays unbound.
5. **sminos hands over.** With the endpoint enrolled, sminos runs no DX2 uplink. RL10 carries:
   - log events both ways, each signed once and resent byte-identical from the signature ledger;
   - lane A RPCs by opId, with `query` and the tombstone;
   - revocations down;
   - the lane A plane and listings up.
6. **Keys.** The machine key and the software device keys are 0600 files in 0700 directories. Step-up behind LocalAuthentication works in an unsigned build (U5).
7. **Live.** The devcloud acceptance VM, cut over with O14's first brief, passes items 2–5.

8. **Namespace grades (OX9).** The endpoint authorizes every forwarded command by its namespace's declared grade, and refuses a namespace with none (default deny). A test sends one command of each grade class through a channel and checks that each is refused below its grade: a view-grade read on another session's checkout, a manage-grade write, `pty.spawn` without the shell right, and a machine-admin setting. A second test shows that an undeclared namespace added to the registry is unreachable remotely until it declares a grade. A machine owner opening a terminal on their own machine needs no step-up; a holder of the shell right who is not a machine owner does; the same-uid local app is exempt.
9. **Either carrier.** The protocol package's RL2 runs unchanged over the hub multiplex and over a direct socket on the tailnet, with one channel suite driven over both. The direct listener binds only to the tailnet interface, runs nothing before the handshake verifies, and rate-limits handshakes (RL §4.1).

**Edges.** Blocked by O2, by O11 for items 2, 3, 5 and 7, and by O3 for item 2's lane B session, whose events reach a `mawsd` with no app attached only through O3's per-client subscriptions (Decision Log 29). Blocks O7, O9 (remote), O12 (RL10 parts), O13 and O14.
**Owner:** desk-sminos-orchestrator. **Contracts.** RL1–RL4, RL7, RL10, OX1.
**Required.** For parent acceptance.
**Status.** executing: its document landed (PR #214, master 1f0fc13ec), eleven milestones with a PR each; M1 to M9 build against its own `EndpointServices` (Decision Log 45); M10 landed (PR #310, master ae73b0546, Decision Log 124); M11 landed (Decision Log 148).

### O11: Hub v2 (desk side) — plan

**Purpose.** The hub stops being a party to trust and becomes a relay and a store of signed things. It:
- keeps the org-state chain (RL5) and refuses forks and rollbacks;
- relays sealed frames between apps and endpoints without being able to read them (Machine DO);
- stores the project log with RL8 signatures (Project DO);
- manages members and devices;
- serves the app's own hub connection (OX8/RL11);
- sends push only to a session's owners.

Through the cutover it keeps today's DX2 and DX6 paths for every machine, and for the phone, until each one moves.

**Acceptance.**
1. **Staging.** `desk-hub-staging` exists with its own Access app and serves as the test org (owner gate: an Access app and its secrets).
2. **The registry.**
   - A successor state is accepted only if it links to the head, and rollback is refused.
   - Members can be added, removed, re-roled and have their email changed.
   - Adding a device takes the member's signature and an admin's countersignature.
   - Provisioning keys are re-issued as delegations, and machine certificates are served beside the state.
3. **The Machine DO** relays RL1 and RL2 frames opaque and under 1 MiB, with DX2's lease, `hb` and generation fencing. A deploy drops channels, and they resume by cursor.
4. **The Project DO** stores v2-signed events (key id, `-v2` domain). It accepts dual-coded events during migration and keeps v1 verification for history. Its conflict rule holds with stored signatures.
5. **Push** goes only to the session owners' devices and says only that a session is waiting or wrote.
6. **The app's connection.** The MAWS app gets past Access (U9) and holds one socket per hub object it uses.
7. **U2 measured.** Sealed-relay latency and throughput, app → Machine DO → endpoint, are measured against RL's targets. Whether a direct tailnet transport is needed is decided and recorded.
8. **The phone never breaks.** Every deploy in this child keeps DX2, DX6 and desk-web's current routes working for the machines and the phone not yet moved.

**Gates.** Deploys go through the desk's pre-approved deploy scripts. Durable Object schema migrations, Access changes and secrets wait for the owner.
**Edges.** Blocked by nothing; it starts beside O1. Blocks O7, O8, O10 (live parts), O13 and O14.
**Owner:** desk-sminos-orchestrator. **Contracts.** RL1, RL5, RL8, OX8/RL11.
**Required.** For parent acceptance.
**Status.** landed: M1 to M8 (PRs #195, #202 to #206, #208, #210), acceptance 1 to 8 run live on staging with item 5's live push carried to O13 (Decision Log 39), and its closing xhigh panel's fixes (PR #212, Decision Log 41); O8's H1, an admin's read of another member's pending devices, landed after it (PR #306, Decision Log 121).

### O12: sminos under the endpoint: owners, words and the lane B observer (desk side) — plan

**Purpose.** sminos stays the project family and the delivery path. It gains the owner model and stops being a network party. In detail:
- every session record carries its owners and its inference route;
- a person's words reach a session only from someone who may drive it;
- to-human messages and asks stay off the hub's log;
- the RPC ledger is keyed by opId;
- the bridge observes lane B sessions without controlling them.

**Acceptance.**
1. **The record.** Every member record carries:
   - `owners` and `ownersProof`;
   - `origin {kind, requester?, spawnerSession?}`;
   - `principal` and `inference`, per RL6, whose `owners` and `principal` admit the token `@machine` (remote layer Decision Log 17).
   Defaults:
   - a terminal session gets `@machine`, its machine's owners as the endpoint module resolves them against signed org state;
   - a `sminos spawn` child copies its parent's owners, proofs and route at spawn and records the parent's session id;
   - records that predate the change read as `@machine`.
2. **Words.**
   - A `human` event is delivered as words only in two cases: the endpoint verified that its signer's member holds `converse` on the target session, or it comes from the machine-attested operator path into a same-machine session that the machine owner owns.
   - Anything else is delivered as a peer message.
   - The check runs again at delivery, against revocations from RL10, so an owed delivery from a removed device never lands as words.
3. **The label.** A `sminos say` from a cut-over machine carries RL9's attestation, built by the endpoint from the record. A receiving session sees "from `<agent>`, owned by `<owner>`" as a peer message.
4. **The ledger.** The RPC ledger is keyed by opId, with `query` and the tombstone over RL10. MW5's upload id becomes the opId.
5. **Local-only.**
   - With RL10 present, sminos runs no DX2, no HMAC coding and no hub enrollment.
   - Without it, on a machine not yet cut over, behaviour is unchanged.
   - Both modes pass the existing sminos and bridge test suites.
6. **To-human off the hub.**
   - On a cut-over machine, `<message>` exports and async asks go to a machine-local store served over RL3, not to the hub's project log.
   - `sminos chat --host` keeps working for the machine's own sessions.
7. **The lane B observer.** Under MAWS's lane B spawn marker, the bridge:
   - honours the marker only in the process MAWS's engine host launched; a `claude` started from inside a lane B session inherits its environment (a command in its Bash, or a `--bg` seat whose supervisor that session started), stays lane A with full control, and a test shows it;
   - posts subagent deltas;
   - holds the `turn.step` reaping gate;
   - refuses every control operation, reporting the controls in `capabilities.controls` as unavailable with the reason "MAWS controls this session";
   - reads `launchedBy` on a remotely spawned session's record and claims the member with its sealed action as `ownersProof`, so the requester owns what it spawned (O7 M4; Decision Log 166).
8. **The mini.** The mini runs this sminos, installed through mini-ops at the owner's request.

**Edges.** Items 1, 4 and 7 start at once. Items 2, 3, 5 and 6 are blocked by O10 (RL10). Blocks O8, O9 and O14.
**Owner:** desk-sminos-orchestrator. **Repos:** doperpowers (sminos plugin) and MAWS (the shared shapes). **Contracts.** RL6, RL8, RL9, RL10, OX10 (provider).
**Required.** For parent acceptance.
**Status.** in flight for items 1, 4 and 7: its execution document is landed (PR #186); the reaping gate waits for U6 and OX10's adoption, and the rest is blocked by O10.

### O13: `web-cards`, the phone (desk side) — plan

**Purpose.** Keep the owner's phone loop through the cut. Desk-web keeps push and the question and permission cards, and nothing else. It now runs as a `web-cards` device: RL2 over WebCrypto, with a WebAuthn assertion on every answer. So the phone keeps answering sessions on machines that took the cutover.

**Acceptance.**
1. **Registration.** The phone registers as a `web-cards` device: a non-extractable WebCrypto P-256 key plus a passkey. It is approved from the owner's Mac and listed in the org state.
2. **A full round trip.** On a cut-over machine, a permission prompt and a question each:
   - push to the phone;
   - show their card contents over the phone's channel;
   - are answered with a passkey gesture.
   The machine checks that the assertion's challenge is the action's digest and that the member owns the session. This holds for one lane A and one lane B session. The push reaches the owner's phone and no other member's device, which O11's staging run could not show live (Decision Log 39).
3. **The class's bound.** Send, steer, spawn, commands and every other action are refused at the endpoint for this class. It may query the outcomes of its own answer acts (`session.answer`, `sminos.answer`, `sminos.decide`) on a session its member owns, as O10 M5's review set (Decision Log 64); lane A's query refusal stands (Decision Log 79).
4. **Side by side.** Sessions on machines not yet moved keep their DX6 cards on the same phone.
5. **The rest freezes.** Desk-web's other views are frozen.

**Edges.** Blocked by O10 and O11. Ships before the cutover of the first machine whose sessions the owner answers from the phone; in the recommended M1, that's the mini. Before O14's B4, desk-web deploys at or after O14 M5a's `org-protocol`, with a way to read the phone's running build (Decision Log 75).
**Owner:** desk-sminos-orchestrator. **Contracts.** RL2, RL4, RL5 (device entry), OX8/RL11 (push registration).
**Required.** For parent acceptance.
**Status.** executing; document landed (`docs/doperpowers/plans/2026-10-27-p5-o13-web-cards.md`, PR #241, Decision Log 69); its M4 needed O10 M7 and M8, both landed (Decision Logs 73 and 77); production's org is rooted and pinned (Decision Log 119).

### O14: Cutover (desk side) — brief per machine

**Purpose.** Move each machine to `mawsd` and RL1 for good without breaking the phone or the logs, then retire the shared org key. The order is the devcloud acceptance VM, then the mini, then the MacBook.

**Acceptance, per machine.**
1. **Re-enrollment.** The machine re-enrolls in place under its name, through a member's code or a delegation. A devcloud VM's ref takes its delegation's id from the seed at first run, and a revoked delegation's VMs re-enroll under a new delegation with a new prefix (Decision Logs 30 and 31). A delegated VM gets its delegation's private key from devcloud's secret store through a v2 provisioning code that O14 builds, carrying `enrollDelegated`'s fields, the redemption token as well as the delegation key (Decision Logs 35 and 45). It is listed in RL5, and executes nothing until the state arrives.
2. **History.** Its history is re-attested once. The app draws its old events as "attested at migration by `<machine>`".
3. **Single-trust inbound.** From the cutover on, DX6 and DX1 execution input is refused. Outbound events are dual-coded until the key retires, so machines not yet moved still receive its events.
4. **The phone.** The phone keeps answering its sessions: through O13 if it's phone-answered, otherwise through DX6 until it moves. A re-enrollment in place ends the machine's DX2 at its redemption, so the phone's loop for that machine pauses until it is listed and cut over, inside a window the owner names: 30 minutes, entered with no session waiting and nothing owed, a card test within 10 minutes of the cutover, and an undo to DX2 within 60 minutes (the remote layer's Decision Log 29; Decision Log 75).
5. **sminos.** Its sminos is local-only (O12). O12 phase 2's sminos is installed on the machine before its endpoint enrolls, since an older sminos's DX2 uplink takes the hub's per-name generation and drops the endpoint's RL1 socket (4409). `hub.json` and the legacy key stay until the final brief, and undoing a cutover is O14's one idempotent sequence, the same before or after sminos's welcome (Decision Log 75, amending Decision Log 51's bare removal of `daemon/identity.json`). The sminos floor for a cutover is 1.27.3: 1.27.0 brought the `sminos daemon owed` that the nothing-owed gate runs (Decision Log 80), and 1.27.3 the operator writer's wait for the upstream's head at a re-enrolment (Decision Log 96). A machine is cut over only once its endpoint's enrollment is org-listed: a MAWS build from master runs an endpoint long before O14 enrolls it, and that alone never takes the machine off the desk hub (Decision Log 55).

**The final brief.** Once the last machine and the phone have moved:
- the org key is retired;
- the hub deletes the wrapped copies;
- the endpoint ignores DX1;
- dual-coding stops;
- DX2 and DX6 leave the hub.

**Gates.** The mini through mini-ops at the owner's request. Any hub migration, Access change or secret waits for the owner. Hub v2's first production deploy runs from steps kept in the repo, in `apps/desk-hub/deploy/` beside O11's staging runners, never from wrappers under `/tmp` (Decision Log 40).
**Edges.** Blocked by O10, O11 and O12. The mini is also blocked by O13 and by mini-ops' sminos upgrade.
**Owner:** desk-sminos-orchestrator.
**Required.** For parent acceptance.
**Status.** document landed (`docs/doperpowers/plans/2026-10-27-p5-o14-cutover.md`, PR #249, Decision Log 75): a shared build, M1 to M5b, and the briefs B0 to B4; the shared build executing.

### O16: The holder — plan

**Purpose.** Harden the engine host as the holder of the holder design (§3.1–§3.3; owner F3, 2026-10-24): a session host per lane B process, the output seq ring and stdin offset, the writer fence, roster re-adoption, and the verified-empty 24-hour release (F2), so an engine host on a server that never idles can be upgraded without ending a session.
**Acceptance.** Acceptance 13's engine-host case on the mini and the VM; the verified-empty release replaces the servers' wall-clock rule, armed through the observer bridge's `turn.step` gate; the `-p` reaping gate is measured (U6); the session host's language and its darwin-arm64 and Linux builds are recorded; OX7 holds with the session host's hop.
**Edges.** Blocked by O3, and by O12 (its item 7, the lane B observer). Blocks O15.
**Contracts.** OX1, OX6, OX7, OX10 (consumer). It keeps O10's lane B action ledger (`src/shared/host/op-ledger.ts`) where the effect lands when it re-homes lane B processes into session hosts (Decision Log 45).
**Required.** For parent acceptance.
**Status.** execution document landed (PR #394, master b91f19d60, Decision Log 167): seven milestones; M0 landed (PR #401, master c46a16289, Decision Log 169), M1 built, M2 and M5 executing, one landing at M6; O12's M4 builds the gate's sminos side against OX10 as adopted.

### O17: The agent's browser with no app, and its remote view — plan

**Purpose.** Give a session's agent a browser on its own machine when no app is attached there, with the render host serving cua as a windowless tab engine, and let a person on another machine see and operate that tab by screencast, so the tab still reaches the session machine's localhost (§6's browser control, survey §7.5; §13 U3, settled by O5 M7: promote, offscreen, `spikes/u4-render-service/FINDINGS.md` §5.6; about 6,100 new or changed lines by O17's document, an estimate and not a promote bar: local 4,475, remote 1,590, range about 4,900 to 7,300).
**Acceptance.** The local stage closes items 1 to 9, the remote stage item 10.
1. **The first browser on a machine with no app.** On a Mac on the daemon shape with the render runtime and no app attached, a lane B session started through the ordinary start path gets the daemon's socket in `CUA_BROWSER_BACKENDS` (a stub engine that comes ready: O5 M5's unresolved e2e), and `session.capabilities` reads `browser: {available: true, viewed: 'screencast'}` before Chromium starts. cua's inventory answers `[]` from the cold broker with `render.starts` 0. cua's real creation sequence (`windows.query`, `windows.create`, `tabs.create`) starts the render host and gets its tab within `CUA_BROWSER_START_MS` (4,000 ms, or the request's smaller remaining budget). The agent navigates to that machine's localhost, attaches, screenshots and closes the tab, and `render.starts` reads 1 afterwards, with no artifact made or fetched before. A rejected peer gets no hello, loads no broker chunk and starts nothing; no capability read, inventory read or app attachment loads the broker, feed or render chunk. This amends O5's acceptance 3 for a machine with a usable runtime; a machine with neither runtime nor app still refuses as O5 ships it. With an app attached, M5's relay is unchanged, and O5 M5's scene 3 is extended: A's own relay works while A's peer reaches neither B's app nor B's render-host backend.
2. **The cold broker is bounded** (draft §M; the review's P2). Admitted-cold connections have a per-session and a daemon budget, cold buffered bytes an aggregate daemon budget, and one connection's pending bytes `CUA_PROMOTION_MAX_BYTES` (1 MiB). The broker stops reading while a write is backpressured and resumes on `drain`. Tests over a real socket: an inventory flood with the reader stopped, and fragmented input across many connections, each holding the daemon at its budget and closing the offender. Promotion: one suppressed hello and one delivered request for a fragmented or coalesced trigger with following bytes; a start timeout answers the triggering RPC with an error and closes; an old asynchronous ready after a handover cannot splice; O5 M5's `PENDING_BUDGET`, extended (8 per session, 64 per daemon), counts peer-root waits and promotions as one counter.
3. **Handover is destructive and generation-fenced.** With an app attaching during the start, an active lease, an in-flight browser command and a concurrent export: the session's epoch advances; every old waiter settles once; cua sees `lost` and retries into the app within its 5 s cycle; the render host's tabs for that session close, and their hold clears only after the drop is confirmed; another session's tabs and the export survive. The same holds through attach, quit and reattach, and for a reused pid with another start. An app leaving returns the path to cold service, never to an automatic Chromium start.
4. **The release fence** (`host.browserWork`; its seam with O16 under Contracts). A session holding a headless tab is released neither by the budget release nor by the reap's verified-empty step while it holds it. At its due time the idle release (the machine role's clock, 10 minutes on a laptop and 24 hours on a server, `host.exitWhenIdle` included) first has `mawsd` close the session's headless tabs, and proceeds once the hold clears without restarting its clock. At zero tabs each release proceeds; one session's close leaves another's hold. The fence is acquired and acknowledged before any zero-to-one tab transition, and a failed fence creates no tab. After an engine-host reconnect, and in an O16 successor, a session adopted before `mawsd`'s first snapshot on that link reads `work.holds.browser: 'unknown'` until the snapshot applies, never empty in between; one adopted later takes the kept snapshot's verdict. The compatibility matrix: an app's tabs, from any build, keep today's release behaviour (at a release an agent tab stays in the panel as the person's tab), by rule and not as a gap; an engine host without `host.browserWork` gets no headless browser, `browser` reading unavailable with the reason "the engine host on <machine> predates browser work holds; it is replaced when idle". The review's test: a legacy app takes over, and a reconnect or adoption happens before any browser-work report.
5. **A retained headless tab closes after recovery** (the review's P1). The creating cua connection is lost, a cua host reconnects and closes the retained last tab, the render seam's hold and `host.browserWork`'s hold both clear, and the render host exits after its quiet period. The app's protection of the person's tabs is unchanged.
6. **The producer ends one subscription on its own** (the review's P2). After a successful `open`, a terminal notice bound to connection, epoch and subscription ends that subscription (`frame_too_large`, a destroyed or suspended view, a producer failure), disposing its pending operations and pressed inputs exactly once. Test: the local viewer over the real machine-local wire, failing after a successful open.
7. **M7's seven conditions** (FINDINGS §5.6; O5 Decision Log "M7's verdict"), each with its test:
   1. Tab views are offscreen at 1280×800 in their nonshown sizing container, main tabs and adopted popups alike, with no window shown and no Dock icon.
   2. Viewer frames come from Electron's frame subscription, one unacknowledged frame per viewer, the newest unsent image kept. A 60 Hz page read by a 2 fps viewer for 60 s keeps at most one unsent source image and one encoded frame per viewer; the image after each acknowledgement is from the latest paint within 250 ms; queues and heap stay flat. Pass 3's scene (the stream across a cua screenshot, detach and reattach) repeats on Mac and Linux.
   3. One bounded macOS offscreen-clock probe (one CI-opt slot, at most 20 minutes, under `spikes/u3-remote-browser/`, not a gate) records its remedy or the limit: about 110 ms click to paint, 100 ms timers near 6 Hz, `requestAnimationFrame` measured.
   4. No `powerSaveBlocker` on the product path.
   5. A uid peer policy for MAWS's cua server on Linux; on darwin a missing addon still fails closed.
   6. `CuaEventScope`: with two consumers on one tab debugger, the cua host receives only what its own lease asked for, and a screenshot gets its frame from its own `Page.startScreencast`, and cua's `Page.enable` at a tab's first use subscribes no frames.
   7. A held tab's footprint is background work, recorded outside the idle scene (Mac physical footprint, Linux RSS; M7 read 134,000 to 178,000 KiB and 0.5 to 0.6 GiB).
8. **Linux reads unavailable, with its reason.** On a Linux VM with the runtime and no app, `browser` reads `{available: false, reason}` naming cua's missing Linux peer policy until cua's owner pins a build with that policy and a two-repo scene passes. MAWS's Linux primitives are proved through the protocol fixture alone, and no artifact claims a real Linux agent browser before that scene. Acceptance 10's browser clause holds on the VM by this refusal.
9. **The machine's cost and boundary** (acceptance 14 and 15; OX7). On the local stage's merge tree, on the mini under CI-opt's hold:
   - `e2e:daemon-idle` three valid runs from a cold daemon start: no session listener, no broker or feed chunk, no new timer.
   - `e2e:daemon-idle-render` three runs with a browser warm-to-idle case: a real cua connector kept across two quiet periods and two 5 s retry intervals; at the window no render child, tab, frame timer, pending broker request or browser-work hold; the resident broker code, the quiescent listener and the connected cua socket counted, never excluded.
   - A kept-session, no-app scene: each enrolled session's one bound, quiescent listener (no timer, no polling, no Chromium), its cost read.
   - Each within the bound in force (99,145 KiB, Decision Log 128) by two of three; `e2e:local-latency-daemon` once within OX7.
   - Every new socket holds RL §5.11: symlink, foreign-owner, direct A-to-B and stale-epoch attempts (replies from a connection no longer selected for the session, or for a request already settled; the engines carry no epoch on the machine-local wire) against both backend roots are refused.
10. **The remote view** (O5's "the agent's browser for a remote session follows U3's spike outcome"; O5 acceptance 7's promoted half). Two profiles, B with no app. A opens B's session and sees the tab B's agent made against B's localhost, inventory and pixels under `view`. After a `machine.browserControl` grant (`approve` on the session, bound to the session and its epoch, revocable on its own and never implied by `view`; a non-owner steps up once per grant), A's input changes the page and the next frame shows it. Also:
    - a `view`-only member sees the tab and never obtains input;
    - a revoked grant drops queued input and releases pressed state before any further input;
    - B's app attaching mid-lease closes A's stream with `engine_changed`, and A then views the app-owned agent tabs through the same producer;
    - another session's tab is unreachable;
    - a slow carrier holds one frame plus 4 KiB and shows the newest frame, frame credit refunded only after draw or drop;
    - a hidden Browser tool closes its feed, not B's tab;
    - a peer without `rl2.browser-screencast` draws a reasoned unsupported view.

    The live mini-to-MacBook surface is O7's scheduled sitting.
**Edges.** Blocked, both stages, by O5's landing 2 (the daemon-owned cua socket and relay, `session.capabilities`, M7's verdict). The local stage's landing is blocked by O16's landing, since the fence edits O16's engine-host files (O17's document, route item R4: `budget.ts`, `work-state.ts`, `idle-release.ts`, `process-table.ts`, `launcher.ts` and others; `holder/reap.ts` tests only). The remote stage is blocked by O7 M5 (the session-bound read open on O7 §N, with the `browser.screencast` kind and `rl2.browser-screencast` reserved); O10 accepted `machine.browserControl` at `approve` on 2026-11-01. A Linux agent browser is blocked by cua's owner (a pinned peer policy and a two-repo scene, cua #242; the broker's protocol behaviours under contract tests, cua #251); the local stage lands without it. Blocks nothing: O7 M5 lands without the browser kind but reserves its name, and O15 does not wait on O17 (Required).
**Contracts.** OX1 (every name additive; `host.browserWork`, `browser.engine` and `rl2.browser-screencast` are capabilities, with the matrix of item 4), OX5 (browser work is not interest; the render host reports none), OX7, OX9 (the engines' commands in a new namespace `browserEngine`, uniform `internal`, so `browser` stays uniform `client`; `host.browserWork` `internal`; the `browser.inventory` feed at `view` in place of a `machine.browserInventory` act; `machine.browserControl` `approve`; `browser.remote.*` `client`, never offered remotely), RL2 (the `browser.screencast` kind), RL4 (the `machine.browserControl` act), RL §5.11. Neither the engine (D-1) nor cua changes for the broker or the event scope.
- **The fence's seam with O16** (O16's answer, 2026-11-01; it replaces the draft §P's adoption and journal text). No generic hold set exists. All three releases pass through one chokepoint, `holdsWork(s)` in `src/engine-host/budget.ts`, which reads `status.work`, a `WorkState` (`src/engine-host/work-state.ts`: `{crons, unknown, descendants}`; `unknown` already counts as work).
  - The budget release reaches it through `hardReasons()`, the idle release through `isIdleRow`, and O16's verified-empty release through `isVerifiedEmpty` (`src/engine-host/holder/reap.ts`).
  - O17 adds a per-session external-hold map to `WorkState`, surfaced as the additive status field `work.holds?.browser`, `'held'` or `'unknown'`. `holdsWork` is true while any entry is present. `budget.ts` folds it into `background_tasks`, with no new `HardReason`. The idle exception (O17's D20): `IdleRelease`'s clock reads the row as if no browser hold were present; at its due time a row held only by the browser hold waits with no timer and emits `host.browserIdle`, `mawsd` drops the session's headless tabs, and the row is reaped right after the status change that clears the hold, its clock not restarted.
  - The map is fed by `host.browserWork`: a full current snapshot, `internal`, sent on `mawsd`'s permanent engine-host link and refused from forwarded callers. A link that ends turns its held entries `unknown` until the next snapshot.
  - Nothing enters O16's checkpoint or journal. `createAdopter` (`src/engine-host/launcher.ts`, beside M3a's `host_gap`) marks a session adopted before the link's first snapshot `browser: 'unknown'` until that snapshot applies; one adopted later (O16 adopts in the background, `adoptAtStart`) takes the verdict of the snapshot the engine host keeps, which is per process, so a successor starts with none. `mawsd` sends that snapshot first on every fresh engine-host link, as it sends `sendInterest(true)` (`src/mawsd/daemon.ts:751`).
  - O16 builds none of this; O17 edits those files after O16 lands.
- **The remote stage's surfaces with O7 and O10.** O17's document (§K, route item R1) is the proposal to O7 M5: the kind's envelope, the session-bound read open, the `browser.inventory` feed and the one-frame backlog exception. O7 M5 owns the session-bound read open, which its own `http` and `document` streams need, and reserves the kind and `rl2.browser-screencast`; O17 M9 builds the browser kind on it after O7 M5 lands, with no joint landing. O10 accepted `approve` with four pins for §K and M9's tests: a non-owner's step-up per grant and per epoch, web-cards devices refused, the act idempotent under the endpoint ledger's `orphanAtOpen` with the grant dying with its epoch, and input re-authorized from the Authorizer's record read.
**Required.** Not for parent acceptance, by the owner's answer of 2026-11-01. Acceptance 10's browser clause holds by O5's refusal (and on Linux by item 8's), and acceptance 4 names no agent browser. O17 carries §6's intent that browser control moves onto the machine past the bar P5 set, and O15 records any stage not yet landed as carried. The owner declined the alternative, reading §5's "everything the panel shows for a local session works for a remote one" as binding on the Browser tool, which would have added the agent's tab viewed and operated from a remote app to acceptance 4 and made O15 wait on both stages.
**Status.** section written from O5's `[cut]` (Decision Log 187); execution document landed (PR #430, master ab0c95788, Decision Log 189): ten milestones, the local stage M1 to M8 as one landing (M7 after O16's landing), the remote stage M9 and M10; executing since O5's landing 2 (Decision Log 191), the local stage's M7 waiting for O16's landing.

### O15: Recomposition and the owner's week — brief

**Purpose.** Verify the parent acceptance as a whole, check that O13's deployment admin device is no longer a root once the app's device is (Decision Log 69), delete main's embedded composition root (§2), and record the owner's verdict (acceptance 16).
**Edges.** Blocked by every other child, O16 included; not by O17, which is not required: O15 records any O17 stage not yet landed as carried.
**Required.** For parent acceptance.
**Status.** deliberately last, blocked by every other child.

## Cross-child contracts

- **RL1–RL10** as the remote layer's Interfaces section states them, lifted whole and binding (RL §Interfaces).
- **OX1 Wire compatibility.** Every hello (app to `mawsd`, `mawsd` to the engine host, endpoint to hub, device to endpoint) keeps the socket preamble's stable fields (`t`, `protocol` as an integer, `token`; `src/shared/host/socket-protocol.ts:6-8`), with `protocol` read as the major, and adds `minor`, `build` and `capabilities` beside them. Peers accept any minor of their major; changes within a major are additive; a removal or rename takes a major, with the old form served for one release. One exception (Decision Log 101): a landing that moves a piece of state's ownership out of the app (O3-D, the spine into `mawsd`) may refuse, within the major, an app's hello that lacks the capability saying it obeys the new owner, since that app would be a second writer; the refused app is told to update, and what it can still write beside the new owner, by hand or in the window before the new owner yields to it, is the transition's stated residual, which the moving landing bounds and measures (Decision Log 163).
- **OX2 Machine-scoped address.** `MachineRef` is a stable id minted once per profile at its first run, by whichever of the app and `mawsd` runs first (`[A-Za-z0-9_-]{16,64}`, at least 128 random bits), kept in `<profile>/machine.json` through one module both use, and never changes, independent of the machine key; RL5's machine entry carries it as `ref`, set at enrollment, immutable and unique, and every contract field named `machine` carries the ref. The enrolled name is shown from the org state. Every machine-bound id travels as `{machine, id}`; layout, drafts and stores key by it; the local machine is a machine like any other. In the renderer an address is the string `<ref>/<id>`, joined and split only by O6's codec, and drafts stay in their session's machine's index, reached through the address (Decision Log 27).
- **OX3 Org project.** `OrgProject {id, name, checkouts: [{machine, path, projectId}]}`, derived from sminos's project identity. Every checkout yields a project: a repository with no remote, or a folder with no repository, is a project of its own on its machine rather than folded into the machine's project, so every project the sidebar shows today stays. Only sessions with no checkout belong to their machine's project. A checkout is a project someone opened on its machine; a session belongs to its machine's project when its directory's org project has no checkout on any visible machine, or when it has no project. `OrgProject.id` is a key enrollment doesn't change (`smns:`, `remote:`, `repo:`, `folder:` or `machine:`), and the record carries `kind` and `smnsId`, sminos's id for joins. Persisted keys survive enrollment (Decision Logs 21 and 27).
- **OX4 Client port.** `{pick, save, confirm, reveal, openExternal, notify, badge, focus}`, called by the daemon through the requesting client; a request with no client answers `noClient` and the caller degrades, never hangs. `openExternal` takes a URL or a path, and `focus` either brings the window forward or requests attention (the dock bounce). `confirm` is a yes/no question parented to the client's window (Decision Log 20).
- **OX5 Interest.** Each client reports its open sessions and subscriptions; the daemon's idle release and visible set read the union.
- **OX6 Session record.** RL6, plus the lane B binding (holder design §3.1) and the inference route.
- **OX7 Local latency budget.** §10. Delta-to-paint is measured as delta to frame: from the engine's write to the end of the frame that renders it on the renderer's main thread, GPU presentation excluded (Decision Log 20).
- **OX8 App ↔ hub.** The app's own hub connection, beside RL2's channels: the org-state chain, signed listings (the sidebar and its toggle), the project log plane (`sminos say` traffic and the project channel's agent rows), push registration and device enrollment. O11 builds the hub half; O6 and O8 consume it; the app verifies RL8 signatures against the org state it holds.
- **OX9 Namespace grades.** Every forwarded namespace declares its grade in the registry: reads of a session's checkout (`fs.read`, `git.diff`, `scm.status`, `artifacts.read`) are `view` on that session; writes (`fs.write`, git mutations, `artifacts.edit`) are `manage`, or `spawn` on the machine; `pty.*` is the machine owners' or an explicit shell right; machine settings, connectors and the catalog's machine half are `machine-admin`. The endpoint refuses an undeclared namespace. The grades are three kinds, and no kind is ranked against another: the session ranks `list` to `manage` in RL §5.5's order; the machine grades `spawn`, `shell` and `machine-admin`; and the routing classes `client` (served by the app, never sent to a daemon) and `internal` (a daemon's own services only), which no caller holds. A command whose grade depends on its input declares a floor and is graded by its effect at the endpoint (Decision Log 20).
- **OX10 The reaping gate (adopted).** desk-sminos-orchestrator proposed it. O12's M4 provides it and O16 consumes it. It binds from O16's execution document's landing (Decision Log 167), U6 (O16's M0) gating it, with the text of that document's Interfaces §E, which O12's M4 builds against: the engine host's own gate server at `gate.sock`, `origin: 'peer'`, `delivery`, `hello.childPid`, a 5 s reconnect with a fresh `hello`, and the settlement below. The text as proposed, in O12's execution document (M4 and Interfaces §C): the engine host serves `MAWS_HOLDER_SOCKET` (0600). The observer bridge sends `hello` and a 5 s heartbeat. The host arms an acknowledged fence with a lease (`arm {fence, leaseMs}`, answered `armed`) and signals only after `armed` and within the lease. While armed, the observer's `turn.step` asks `turn {fence, origin}` and gets `proceed` or `stop`. With no answer, it proceeds only after the lease has expired plus one heartbeat. A stopped turn's trigger stays owed. That needs a delivery settlement correlated with the turn's verdict, because today sminos's `deliver_next` settles an inbox delivery when `push_live` hands it to the engine, before `turn.step` decides. O16 defines that settlement when it adopts OX10, and O12 builds the sminos side. O16 defines the settlement: sminos defers settling a peer delivery only while the observer holds an armed fence, settles it on `proceed` or the lease's expiry, and keeps it owed on `stop`. A session on which no observer was ever seen may be released on verified-empty alone, on both roles; an observer seen and then silent holds it (Decision Log 167).
- **The protocol package.** RL2's handshake and frames, RL4's canonical action, RL5's org state and RL8's signatures are one package with shared test vectors, `apps/org-protocol` in the `apps/*` workspace. O11 creates it with RL5 and RL8 and O10 extends it with RL2 and RL4. It is imported by the hub, `mawsd`, the app's main and desk-web's WebCrypto build. No consumer reimplements them.
