- O11 has no MAWS dependency and starts on day one beside O1; O12's data model (owners, origin, the principal and inference fields, the opId ledger, the lane B observer) starts at once.
- O10's live acceptance needs O11's staging hub; O12's local-only and words-rule parts need O10's RL10.
- O13 ships before the mini's cutover, since the owner answers the mini's sessions from the phone. The MacBook's own sessions are local to its app and need no cutover in M1.
- O12 and O14 change the doperpowers repository as well as MAWS, cross-repo as MW1–MW5 were: MAWS lands first each time, and every new field stays optional.

**Milestones the owner sees.**
- **M1** (O1, O2, O6, O9, O10–O13; the VM's cutover, then the mini's): every session on the MacBook, the mini and the VM in one sidebar, terminal sessions controllable from MAWS, the phone on the new protocol for the mini, and MAWS's own sessions local as today.
- **M2** (O3, O7, the MacBook's cutover; O4 and O5 lighting their capabilities as they land): MAWS sessions at full depth on any machine, outliving the app.
- **M3** (O8, O16, O14's final brief, O15): the org interface, upgrades that end nothing, the shared org key retired, and the owner's week.

## Risks

| Risk | Mitigation |
|---|---|
| The cut degrades daily local use | Every step keeps the app working (survey §3); OX7 stops a step that slows it; the e2e groups run on the daemon shape since O3-B's landing (Decision Log 137; from O2 only the supervisor, unit and latency groups did, Decision Log 101) |
| The wire freezes once main's commands cross a process boundary (settings went v14 to v18 in five days) | OX1 lands first, in O1 |
| S3 is one large step | O3 decomposes at dispatch; S3 moves code verbatim behind O1's seams; two composition roots let the owner's profile flip back |
| Tests or dev runs hit the owner's daemon | One daemon per profile; non-default profiles get private daemons (§2) |
| Retrofitting authorization onto 300 forwarded commands | OX9 declared from O1, enforced in O10 before any namespace crosses a channel |
| Two trust models drift | One document (RL); desk-side children own its code |
| Phone loop broken mid-cutover | O13 before the first phone-answered machine's cutover; O11 keeps DX2 and DX6 for every machine and the phone not yet moved |
| Live tests against the owner's hub | A second deploy, `desk-hub-staging`, with its own Access app, made once at the owner's hand (O11's first step); it is also acceptance 12's test org |
| Acceptance 14's memory bound is tight | Decision Log 57's early check at O2 M5 (07b91743e; mini runs 40533404486, 40535972777 and 40549288255, two more invalid on load alone and agreeing within 0.7%) reads `mawsd` alone, idle with no hosts, at a median of 26481 KiB. With the engine host's 64481 KiB that is 90962 KiB of the 96721 KiB bound: about 5.6 MiB of headroom before M9 adds the relay's buffers and the interest union. O2 M9 measures the daemon shape. If it reads over, look first at `mawsd`'s own 13 MiB above the runtime floor (13153 KiB), what it loads at start O2 M9 measured the daemon shape at 94947 KiB of the 96721 (mawsd 28865 with an adopted upstream, about 2.4 MiB over the mawsd-alone check, plus the engine host's 66081; mini run 40606738519): 1.8% headroom, about M1's run-to-run spread, and local latency's p50 at 25.9 of 26.45 ms. O2 lands on it, and a later miss on noise falls to the two-of-three rule. The first lever is fewer eager imports in mawsd's graph; At landing the bound was re-derived on the landing tree, 101113 KiB and 1.11 s, and the daemon shape read 97683 and 99219 KiB, about 1.9% to spare (Decision Log 107, a tracker row); after the mini's reboot it was re-derived again, 99145 KiB and 1.12 s, and the A-B-A case reads the daemon shape against the detached supervisor inside one job, x1.452 of the x1.5 allowed (Decision Log 128). O3, which moves services toward the daemon, budgets against this bound before it adds to either process. |
| Production's org root is a software key until O8 | The remote layer's Decision Log 28 bounds it: a durable 0600 keys file outside sync and backup scopes, confirmations read from `/dev/tty` so an agent's shell signs nothing, the recovery key only offline; O8 revokes the deployment admin device once the app's device is a root, and O15 checks it (Decision Log 69) |

## Deferred

- The encrypted recent tail on the hub (lid-closed reading): a later child (owner, 2026-10-26).
- Account-managed inference through a server-side gateway, and with it who may drive another person's session (owner, 2026-10-26).
- Signed builds, Enclave keys, notarization and updates (C12 P1): when the Apple Developer ID arrives.
- Shipping MAWS to a second member.
- The MAWS web port (class `web`).
- Name reuse for an enrolled machine, such as a replaced mini, whose name stays bound to its first ref (Decision Log 23). A devcloud VM on a delegation certificate already frees its name when the certificate retires (Decision Log 24).

## Surprises and discoveries

- The engine's remote-tools channel is gated to managed cloud workers, so client-bound tools go through MAWS's own tool server (RL §3.1).
- Claude Code 2.1.311's "SSH mode" strings are a Desktop entrypoint marker, config metadata and a telemetry name; there is no session service to reuse (remote-architecture's note).
- Moving main freezes the wire: the fingerprint leaves main out on purpose (survey §7.1).
- Anthropic's terms bar making a personal account available to anyone else (identity §3), which bounds what co-owners can do until inference is managed per account.
- Lifting ids to the machine dimension (O6 M2) has a class neither the lowerer's refusal nor the coverage snapshot catches: an id carried in a plain string field, or a compound id built on main's bare session id. Four were silent with one machine: `session.takeOver.paneId` (the pause-first takeover never suspended the CLI), mark ids and engine event ids (Mark read matched nothing, side threads' later summaries were dropped), and session ids inside artifact content (dashboard labels read "by Claude"). O6 carries the check into M3 and M4; any child that builds an id on a session id or carries one in a string checks it at the boundary too.
- The endpoint's Notifier (presence counts, notify's waiting and settled) is fed only by RL10's pending, and sminos's pending set holds only bridge-raised requests, which are lane A's. Lane B's broker items reach the endpoint only inside a plane B subscription, so with no phone subscribed a lane B session shows no count and raises no waiting push. O13 M5 found it (2026-10-28); it breaks O13 acceptance 2's lane B push leg and would have met the mini at O14's B2. O10's executor feeds the same Notifier from the engine host's broker items for every listed lane B session, subscribed or not, restated whole on each engine-host reconnect, bounded with no polling, additive on the wire; it lands in its own PR (or in O10 M10 if it needs O2) before O14's B2. It landed as PR #272 (master 96b86b5c9, Decision Log 90). O13 M5 lands with lane A proven and lane B's push leg open for that fix, and its fake sminos drops the lane B pending entries that modelled a contract sminos does not have.

## Decision Log

1. **2026-10-26, owner.** Desk folds into MAWS; MAWS is the one interface, in the org shape; Desk Web pauses; the web port comes later; sessions belong to their spawner, cooperation runs through agents.
2. **2026-10-26, owner, round 1.** The org is the owner alone for months, with the people model built in; an Apple Developer ID is coming; a remote session's controller lives on its machine; the phone loop stays; agent messages are today's `sminos say` with an owner label; the sidebar shows one's own sessions with others behind a toggle; the lid-closed tail is a later child; an offline recovery key; no step-up on routine approvals of one's own session.
3. **2026-10-26, owner, through remote-architecture.** Per-session visibility like a shared document; shares at view, converse or approve; machine owners' grants; app first.
4. **2026-10-26, owner.** Keep this design's credential model; account-managed inference comes in a later phase.
5. **2026-10-26, the architect.** The endpoint is a module of `mawsd`, not a process; the engine host stays separate (RL Decision Log 6).
6. **2026-10-27, the architect.** Main's services go to a services host that is the engine host's single client (survey §3), not into the supervisor. Rejected: the merge, which puts synchronous SQLite on the supervisor's loop and rewrites every single-client row.

7. **2026-10-27, desk-sminos-orchestrator's review, adopted.** M1 includes the mini (with O13 before its cutover); O11 starts on day one; O10's live parts wait for O11; the app ↔ hub contract is OX8; one protocol package owned by O10; to-human messages assembled per machine over channels; a staging hub; U9. Its text for O10–O14 is the children's sections.

8. **2026-10-27, the critique's round, adopted** (doperpowers:critique, converged): the holder is scheduled, not assumed; namespace grades (OX9); one daemon per profile, two composition roots until M2, and a `mawsd` command line; O3 owns the data migration; the tailnet is a first-class carrier of RL2 (RL at 65d7fa11e); O7 no longer waits for O4 and O5; one Electron process or two is U4's to decide by a stated criterion; `MachineRef` is a minted id; OX1 keeps the socket preamble; per-role idle release; acceptance 2, 4, 7 and 12 reworded and 13–15 added; `show_pane` follows the sending device.
9. **2026-10-27, the architect.** The holder is child O16 in P5, not a later phase: an engine host with live sessions on a server may never idle out, so without re-adoption an upgrade either never takes effect or ends every lane B session. Rejected: deferring it, which the critique first proposed and conceded.
10. **2026-10-27, the architect.** O6 is verified against two daemons on one Mac, each with its own `MachineRef` and `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, not through a development adapter on the old hub. Rejected: the adapter, which would be thrown away.

11. **2026-10-27, owner.** The design is approved. The project channel shows question and permission cards inline. The MacBook releases an idle session 10 minutes after the last interest, and servers after 24 hours. Release must be strict: no background Bash job, subagent, Monitor, cron or MCP task (§2, O2).
12. **2026-10-27, the architect: the verification.** The design was reviewed by doperpowers:critique (converged, Decision Log 8–10) and by desk-sminos-orchestrator (Decision Log 7). The remote layer had its own adversarial review and two code reviews. Each plan child's execution document gets doperpowers:adversarial-reviewer's buildability review before it executes. Each child's branch gets doperpowers:review-code at the rung its execution document names, medium by default and xhigh for O3.
13. **2026-10-27, owner, under O11.** The staging hub is approved. desk-sminos-orchestrator creates `desk-hub-staging` on the owner's Cloudflare account with the stored Access setup tokens: a second Worker with its own Access apps and its own push key, reusing the service-token API token. The owner signs in to it once and runs `cloudflared access login` for it once.
14. **2026-10-27, the architect, from remote-architecture's check against the code and the holder design (PR #184).** Strict release names its sources (§2, O2 item 3). Ambient tasks don't count, keeping E10's rule. A workflow, the engine's fifth task kind, counts. Crons are read from Stop's `session_crons` through a new Stop hook callback, because nothing reads them today. An unreadable state is narrowed to three cases. Rejected: counting ambient tasks, which would keep any session with an auto-started watcher from ever releasing. Flags O2 and O16.
15. **2026-10-27, the architect.** Each child gains Required and Status fields, per the composite template of doperpowers:decomposing 7.208.0, which this spec had followed without them. No decision changes.
16. **2026-10-27, the architect, under O11, O12 and O16, from desk-sminos-orchestrator's execution documents (branch p5-o11-o12-plans, d012d84c9).** The protocol package is `apps/org-protocol`. O11 creates it, because O11 starts first, and O10 extends it. It uses WebCrypto only and imports with `.ts` extensions so Node loads it unbundled. O12 follows RL6's split of `principal` and `inference` and its `@machine` token (remote layer Decision Log 17, PR #185 landed at 7fcf43afd), so O12's acceptance 1 is reworded. The lane B marker (`MAWS_LANE=B` from `buildEnvironment` in `src/engine-host/launch.ts`) holds only in the process the engine host launched. sminos's `launch_env` copies `os.environ` into every `claude --bg` it starts, and a lane B session's Bash passes its environment to any `claude` it runs, so a marker read from the environment alone would silence the controls of sessions MAWS never drove. OX10, the reaping gate, is added as proposed, with a stopped turn's trigger kept owed. Flags O10, O12 and O16.
17. **2026-10-27, the architect, under O11 and O12, at their execution documents' landing (PR #186, master 1068fe922).** Under O11: RL1 carries DX2's log-plane vocabulary unchanged beside the channel multiplex, so the Project DO's lease, backlog and writer rule stay. Rejected: a new log-plane protocol, which would fork the Project DO. Its buildability review's eight findings are adopted, among them a v2 log signature that binds org and project, a state cache that fails closed, Access admission that follows the org state, and RL1's hibernation state tested across eviction. M7 settles U9 by a spike and doesn't touch the MAWS app. Under O12: a spawned child's provenance travels with its launch as a pending record the child claims once (`SMINOS_LAUNCH`). The controller lane is current state, kept apart from provenance. opIds get a replay horizon (`expired`, `opCreated`), and `opCreated` is asked of RL4. OX10 is revised to an acknowledged fence with a lease, so a host never signals while the observer believes a turn may proceed. Phase 2's milestones are written when O10 fixes RL10. O12's re-review of the folded milestones is still out, and its findings land on the same document.
18. **2026-10-27, the architect, under O10 and O12, from the remote layer's Decision Log 18 (PR #187, master 1c5637466).** RL4 gains `opCreated`. It is set once with the `opId`, kept across reseals, and covered by the seal and the canonical action. An op older than `OP_HORIZON = KEPT_S − 3600` s is refused `expired`. An `opCreated` more than 60 s later than `created` is malformed. `query {opId, opCreated}` gains the answers `expired` and `unavailable`. O10's acceptance names the horizon, and the app's outcome-unknown states (O7) follow RL4. Flags O7, O10 and O12.
19. **2026-10-27, the architect, under O12 and O16, at O12's second buildability round (PR #188, master 8af7513de).** O12 keeps provisional ownership out of the published log. The launch claim is SessionStart's first act. The lane B verdict has three states, and an unresolved verdict stays an observer. `opCreated` is required with every opId. The round found that OX10 as proposed can't keep a stopped turn's peer message owed: `deliver_next` settles at push, before `turn.step` decides. OX10's adoption therefore includes a settlement correlated with the verdict, which O16 defines and O12 builds. Flags O16.
20. **2026-10-27, the architect, under O1, at its execution document (master a768c31ef).** O1's nineteen residue decisions are accepted as its document records them. Among them: one gone moment per page instance for all per-client state; O1 retags no production namespace; the forwarding seam carries the client (`req.client`, `clientGone`); protocol 3 with protocol 2 served for one major; additive means new commands, events and optional fields only, so an added enum member is breaking; a tolerant reader at cross-build receivers, the one exception to charter §12's never-transform rule; and one committed wire manifest. Contracts: OX4 gains `confirm`. OX7 is measured as delta to frame. OX9's vocabulary has three kinds that are never ranked against each other, a condition the architect added so that O10 can't rank `internal` below `list`. Charter X2's owners gain `machine`. OX1's proof at O1 is two registries a minor apart over the real socket in one process, because no earlier protocol-3 build exists. The real two-build check waits for the first change that bumps `WIRE_MINOR`. O1's review rung is high, not Decision Log 12's medium default: every later child builds on OX1's rule and the forwarding seam. Rejected: medium, for a panel-sized branch at a compatibility boundary.
21. **2026-10-27, the architect, under O6, O2, O3 and O11, from O6's planner (a `[parent-impact]` and three overturns).** The edge: O6's two-machine check needs machine services that answer the sidebar's main-owned reads (`session.list`, `session.projects.list`, `session.digests`, `attention.list`, drafts), and neither a detached supervisor nor O2's `mawsd` serves them; only O3's services host does. O6 stays blocked by O1 only. Its stand-in is a second profile's app serving its machine services over a same-uid socket on protocol 3, which a packaged build can't serve. Its serve adapter is deleted at O3, whose acceptance now reruns the scene on the daemon shape. Rejected: blocking O6 on O3, which would leave the renderer's machine dimension unverified until the largest child lands. Ids are addresses in the renderer, lifted and lowered at its client by branded-schema identity, with a coverage test for unbranded id fields, one codec, and a loud failure for a payload that mixes machines. Rejected: §3's id-to-machine map, which needs the same schema walk plus a mutable index that misses an id seen before its record. `MachineRef` is minted once and never changes, independent of the machine key, because the key moves to the Secure Enclave with signed builds. RL5's machine entry gains `ref`, set at enrollment and immutable after (asked of the remote layer and O11). Rejected: the key's fingerprint, which would change at enrollment and again at the Secure Enclave move. OX3: every checkout yields a project on its machine, and only sessions with no checkout go to the machine's project, keeping acceptance 1's every project present. Persisted keys survive enrollment, because sminos's repository id includes the org. Flags O2 (mints the ref), O3, O10 and O11.
22. **2026-10-27, the architect, correcting Decision Log 21's reasoning, from the remote layer's Decision Log 19 (PR #189, master f94f56b8c).** The machine key stays a 0600 file under RL §2.3, because a headless daemon can't use the Secure Enclave. So the Enclave move concerns device keys, unless U4 makes `mawsd` a signed Electron process. The ref's independence stands on the enrollment and rotation grounds: a key change keeps the ref. RL5 fixes the ref's format and its rules. Every contract field named `machine` carries the ref, never the name. The enrollment MAC covers the ref, and a contested ref is refused for every claimant. O6's codec and O2's mint follow it. No decision changes.
23. **2026-10-27, the architect, under O11, O12 and O14, from the remote layer's Decision Log 20 (PR #190, master 06da0ef8f).** Log writers stay `<name>/…`. RL5 gains an append-only `machineNames: {name, ref}[]`, so a name is held under one ref for the life of the org, and RL8 verifies a machine's event only when that binding matches the signer. The visible costs are accepted for now. A used name never passes to another machine, so a replacement mini or a recreated VM takes a new name, and a rename splits the machine's log history. Reuse after retirement is deferred, because no machine is retired in this initiative. Rejected for now: name transfer, which needs a retirement transition and the log position of each retirement.
24. **2026-10-27, the architect, under O10, O11 and O14, from the remote layer's Decision Log 21 (PR #191, master a2aeed0e0).** Retirement and revocation are distinct. Revocation is for a key that may be compromised, and it is retroactive. Retirement is for a key that leaves honestly: it records `{keyId, notAfter}`, and events stamped at or before `notAfter` verify for good. The anchor is the signer's own timestamp, which is trusted only because a retired key is destroyed. A retired key found leaked is revoked. A delegation certificate can be retired by its own delegation key, so `devcloud vm delete` retires its VM unattended, and a retired certificate frees its name for a recreated VM. The delete hook lives in the devcloud plugin and is advisorily O14's, built with the VM's cutover. Decision Log 23's deferral narrows to enrolled machines.
25. **2026-10-27, the architect, under O10, O11 and O12, from the remote layer's Decision Log 22 (PR #192, master bf7e6233a).** A retired entry is `{keyId, pub, notAfter, holder}`. Dropping a retired key revokes it, and a key enters `retired` only in the step where it stops being current. Every v2 event signs its `created`, and retirement compares against that, never the hub's `ts`. A delegation's expiry retires its certificates. The enrollment MAC's encoding (`smns-enroll-mac-v1`) and the `contested` and `name-retired` verdicts are fixed. No P5 text changes.
26. **2026-10-27, the architect, under O8 and O12.** O12 M3's MAWS half landed: `MAWS_LANE=B` in `buildEnvironment` (PR #194, master 0de73723e). Its gate was run 40430118784, whose tree equals the head, and master's moves since were docs only. It changes nothing until sminos reads the marker. From the remote layer's Decision Log 23 (PR #193, master 8a944a589): a revoked key never becomes current again. Only a signer holding the whole chain can check that, so it is the admin's app, built in O8, the child that edits the org state. remote-architecture named O7; the Org page is O8's. The hub and every machine that saw the revocation also refuse such a state from their own record.
27. **2026-10-27, the architect, under O6, O2, O3, O8 and O9, from O6's execution document (master bc8eead77).** O6's document landed: six milestones, each needing O1, after two buildability rounds that converged. Its residue is accepted, and four items extend P5's contracts. (1) The identity file. A profile's `MachineRef` is minted once at its first run, by whichever of the app and `mawsd` runs first, into `<profile>/machine.json` (`{version: 1, ref, mintedAt}`, 0600). It is published by linking a complete temporary file, so the race between the two needs no shared lock, and it is never rewritten; a malformed file stops startup. One module with no Electron import, `src/shared/machine/identity-file.ts`, owns it for main and `mawsd`, and whichever of O2 and O6 lands first creates it. O2's draft had put the file under `<profile>/mawsd/`. The profile's root wins because the ref belongs to the profile in either shape, and the app mints it before `mawsd` exists. (2) OX3's checkout is a project someone opened on its machine (`origin: 'user'`). A session sits under its machine's project when its directory's org project has no checkout on any visible machine, or when it has no project, which keeps today's rule that only opened projects are rows. `OrgProject.id` is a key enrollment doesn't change (`smns:`, `remote:`, `repo:`, `folder:` or `machine:`), and the record gains `kind` and `smnsId`, sminos's id kept for joins. Rejected: persisting sminos's ids, which change at enrollment. One visible consequence: subfolder projects of one repository merge into that repository's row. (3) Drafts stay in their session's machine's index, reached through the address. Rejected: a client-side draft store. (4) Four id kinds are lifted to addresses (`AppSessionId`, `ProjectId`, `PtyId`, `AttentionId`). The engine's `SessionId`, event ids, ids inside one session and artifact ids are not, and path-only commands take an explicit machine. Rejected: lifting every machine-scoped id, which rewrites every engine frame on the delta path OX7 measures. `machine.identity` is the first production `machine`-owned command and bumps `WIRE_MINOR`. If it is master's first bump, O6's executor tells the architect at M1, and O1's two-build check (the tracker's row) is scheduled then. The new `e2e:machines` group starts on the self-hosted Macs and joins the hosted list only through gate-offload's admission rule. Flags: O8 joins the hub and the project log by `smnsId`. Until O10 supplies the enrollment, O9's terminal sessions agree with MAWS's project key by the rule, not by id. O3 and O7 own notifications, the badge and the attention shortcut across machines. The taste decisions T1 to T5 (the machine chip, an offline machine's rows, the machine's row, an org row spanning machines, an offline machine's tab) go to the owner and are built provisionally until answered; T6, the toggle for others' sessions, is O8's.
28. **2026-10-27, the owner, under O6, answering Decision Log 27's taste decisions.** The machine chip is `@name` in secondary text, on other machines' rows only; this Mac's rows look as they do today. An offline machine's rows grey in place, with "offline" where the chip was, and its kept tabs grey with the content "<name> is offline". Sessions in folders nobody opened sit under one row per machine, named after it, after the projects. A project row checked out on several machines shows no machines; its session rows do. O6's document records them as built forms. Rejected: a chip on every row, sinking or folding offline rows, the "Other sessions" wording, machine rows first, and chips on the project row.
29. **2026-10-27, the architect, under O2, O10 and O3, from O2's execution document (master d35bfd612).** O2's document landed: nine milestones, each needing O1, after two buildability rounds that converged; its branch review rung is high. Its residue is accepted, with two narrowings and two extensions. Until O3, `mawsd` is the engine host's client per app attachment and passes the app's ready line through, so no lane B event reaches `mawsd` while no app is attached. Rejected: one always-ready connection now, which loses E10's undelivered tail. `mawsd enroll` and `mawsd keys` answer "no endpoint module" until O10. A fourth unreadable state joins §2's three, conditional on O2's Stop-hook probe: crons stay unknown through an engine-started turn with no tool call, for the keepalive's window, if `-p` re-arms there. The pty host's commands and events join the wire manifest, since the pty host becomes `mawsd`'s build. The edge the first narrowing leaves: O10's acceptance 2 resumes a lane B session, whose events reach a `mawsd` with no app only through O3's per-client subscriptions, so that half of item 2 waits for O3. Rejected: O10 building its own event source, which O3 would replace. Rulings on O2's asks. The Stop-hook probe runs on the mini through a gate job or on Linux, never on this MacBook, where an engine run can reach the Keychain. The engine host keeps E10's `refuse token` line, which names its protocol, build and live count before authentication, to the same uid only. That uid can read the token anyway, so acceptance 15 is narrowed to no session data before authentication; O2's own sockets close a wrong token silently. Settings version 19 is O2's (`supervisor.shape`, `daemon` by default and by migration, so the owner's profile moves to the daemon shape when O2 lands). X6's initialize gains the host's Stop hook callback (charter Decision Log, this date). The release keyed on interest runs while the app is open too, as §2 states, so a session with no tab is released after the idle time while MAWS is open. The acceptance VM, M8's TCC probe (with the owner present) and the mini's measured runs are arranged when the executor reaches them. O2's seven questions for O10 went to desk-sminos-orchestrator, with the architect's answers to two: the lane B edge above, and a remote viewer's subscription counts as interest (OX5).
30. **2026-10-27, the architect, under O8, O2, O10 and O14, from the remote layer's Decision Logs 24 and 25 (PR #199, master d0728d67c) and O10's answers to O2's seam questions.** Revocations live in the signed state: RL5 gains an append-only `revoked: {keyId, holder}[]` for every key class, every admin retirement and revocation is a state change, delegations are immutable in `id`, `pub` and `prefix`, and dropping a delegation revokes it. So a check against the predecessor sees a revoked key made current again, and O8's acceptance no longer rests on holding the whole chain. O8 gains five duties: after a root transition it offers the superseded states' revocations; it warns before dropping a delegation, since that revokes the delegation's certificates and their history; it re-signs a re-enrolled machine's grants, showing each grant before it signs, since the hub that serves them isn't trusted; it refuses a delegation prefix that equals or nests in another or covers an existing name; and it renews a delegation by extending `expires` while it is live, a lapsed one being replaced by a new delegation with a new prefix. A grant is `{machine, machineKey, member, rights, version}`. A daemon that loses its state keeps its ref, because the ref lives in `machine.json` outside `mawsd/` (Decision Log 27), and re-enrolls with a new key under which every earlier grant is refused. For O2: the grant ledger sits beside the machine key in `mawsd/endpoint/`, a daemon holding only one of the two starts unenrolled, and `<profile>/mawsd/` is excluded from backups, while `machine.json` stays in them. For O14 and O2's M8: a devcloud VM's ref is `<delegation id>_<random>`, 32 lowercase hex digits from the seed, then at least 128 random bits, inside RL5's 64 characters, so the identity module takes an optional prefix at first run. From O10's answers (desk-sminos-orchestrator), folded into O2's document by its author: the endpoint starts once `mawsd`'s sockets are up, not after a host's first connect, since parked hosts would leave the machine unreachable. The seam gains `machine.kind`, a `hold` that alone keeps a private daemon from its idle exit, a binary frame path with its own `documents.open` channel range, and an attachment signal. A remote viewer never takes pty credit. `mawsd enroll` reads its code from stdin or a hidden prompt, never argv, and `mawsd endpoint <cmd>` passes through to the endpoint so O10 adds commands without touching O2. `daemon.restart` is machine-admin, `daemon.endpoint` and `host.interest` stay internal, and `session.setKeep` stays manage.
31. **2026-10-27, the architect, under O8 and O14, from the remote layer's Decision Log 26 (PR #200, master ca99cca27).** A delegation's prefix and id are each used once in the life of the org; a revoked one stays reserved through its `revoked` entry, reversing the remote layer's Decision Log 25 on reuse. Dropping a delegation moves its certificates' retired keys into `revoked`. An enrolled machine's ref never takes a delegation's `<id>_` shape, which a 22-character ref minted without a prefix (Decision Log 27) can't. O8 refuses to sign a delegation whose prefix or id was used before; devcloud (O14) takes a new prefix after a revocation.
32. **2026-10-27, the architect, under O2, O10 and O1, at O2's revision (master 25b70c734).** O2's document carries Decision Log 30's seam and state, plus a focused review round whose three blockers were fixed. First, the park clock arms exit-when-idle only while no app is attached and nothing holds interest, so a session that a remote viewer holds is never released. Second, the seam carries `documents.onClosed` and `onUpstream` with a generation number, so the endpoint hears its document channels end. Third, local-only status fields are optional in the one registry schema, so a redacted `daemon.status` still validates. A remote `pty.attach` is forced to `credit: false`, additive under O2's one wire bump. One extension of O1's grade rubric, recorded here: `daemon.status` (`list`) and `daemon.restart` (`machine-admin`) are machine-owned commands remote callers reach, an exception to the rubric line that a main-only command is `internal`. O2's executor changes O1's `grades.test.ts` internal set when it builds them. What `daemon.status` returns to a remote caller carries no paths, log location, tailnet address or key material: those fields are marked local-only, and a test fails an unmarked path field. O10 holds its own `status()` to the same rule (desk-sminos-orchestrator).
33. **2026-10-27, the architect, under O1 and every child OX7 binds, from O1's M1.** OX7's baseline is recorded on the mini at O1's M1 (branch commit 12bd0b3b2; runs 40440185564, 40440764482 and 40441045572): delta to frame p50 24.45 ms and p95 37.56 ms, round trip p50 0.9 ms and p95 1.3 ms, the median of three runs. The spreads, 0.33 and 0.71 ms for delta to frame and 0 and 0.1 ms for round trip, sit well inside the 2 and 5 ms budget, so the bench resolves it and U1 is answered step by step against this baseline. Most of delta to frame is a batching cadence upstream of the frame, not the hops between processes. The baseline lands with O1's branch, in `test/support/local-latency.ts`.
34. **2026-10-27, the architect, under O8 and O11, from O11 M3 (PR #203, master 5082abf48).** The hub's Registry keeps the org-state chain, members and devices. One gap is O8's to settle before acceptance 12: no hub route carries a non-admin member's endorsed new device to an admin for countersigning. It can't arise in a one-person org. O8's plan either builds that flow, with the route asked of O11, or has an admin enroll a member's device directly, and says which.
35. **2026-10-27, the architect, under O14 and O10, from O11 M4 (PR #204, master de9f98697).** The hub now takes v2 enrollments and delegation certificates, but no v2 provisioning code exists yet. A delegated VM needs its delegation's private key at first run, and only O11's fake VM gets one, handed in directly. O14 builds the code (`smns-provision-v2.` or an equivalent) with the acceptance VM's cutover: devcloud's seed carries it from its secret store, and the VM's endpoint (O10) redeems it. Its codec goes in `apps/org-protocol` beside `smns-enroll-v2.` (advisory). Rejected: a hub-issued provisioning code, because the hub never holds a delegation's private key. Flags O10 and O14.
36. **2026-10-27, the architect, under O10, O8 and O3, from O1 M4's 29 grading calls (O1's Decision Log, branch p5-o1 at 36215b896).** All 29 are accepted, including `session.bash` as `shell`, content-free machine reads as `list`, and `engine.sessions`, `session.search` and `attention.list` as `view`. Four rulings settle what O1 left to O10. First, `session.acquire`'s resume under the session's stored spec stays `converse`. Strict idle release ends most sessions' processes, so a resume that took `spawn` would make a `converse` grant lapse whenever its session goes idle. A resume that changes the spec takes the grade of the change it makes, and the resumed process still runs as the session's spawner. Second, `session.memoryAppend`'s `user` target, the machine-wide `~/.claude` memory, is `machine-admin`. Third, `session.update`'s reader fields stay at `view`, but today they are columns of the shared session row (`src/main/index/migrations.ts:38`, `register.ts:27-35`), and drafts live in the same index. A second viewer marking a session read would clear its owner's badge. O8 keeps reader state and drafts per member, keyed by session and member in the machine's index, so a person's devices share one entry and no two people share one. O8's acceptance gains it. Rejected: keeping them in the client's profile, which splits one person's devices. Fourth, the five bridged commands only main sends (`fs.scan`, `fs.bind`, `fs.unbind`, `maws.artifact.captured`, `maws.download.recorded`) become main-only at O3, and with that `internal`. Until then they stay `machine-admin`. Flags O3, O8 and O10.
37. **2026-10-27, the architect, under O13 and O6, from O11 M6 (PR #206, master db987e8bc).** The Project DO's `machines` frame, which desk-web's project view draws, doesn't list machines that connect over RL1. Their presence reaches only the grid's `machines_online` and the relay's `presence` frame. This is accepted. Desk-web's views other than the cards freeze at O13 (its acceptance 5), so a cut-over machine drops out of desk-web's machine list while its cards keep working over the phone's channel. MAWS's sidebar takes machines from the org state and relay presence (OX8, O6), never from this frame. Rejected: teaching the frame RL1 machines, which would extend a frozen view. Flags O13.
38. **2026-10-27, the architect, under O11, O8 and O13, from O11 M7 (PR #208, master cccfd1aae).** U9 is settled. The app gets past Access through Access's login transfer, the flow `cloudflared access login` uses: the system browser, plus a transfer token the app polls. The hub gains no device principal and no device header, because Access turns the app's token into the assertion the Worker already verifies. Rejected: a per-device service token minted at enrollment, which adds an Access credential per device. Two costs are accepted. The transfer uses Cloudflare endpoints that are undocumented, though they are `cloudflared`'s own. And until a renewal without a browser is shown, each device's sign-in costs one browser trip per Access session, 720 h on staging (the Access app's `session_duration`). If the transfer endpoints break, per-device service tokens are the fallback. The Registry socket serves apps with `?app=1`: state notices plus listings in pages of at most 256 KiB. `web-cards` opts in the same way when the phone needs listings. O8's sign-in builds the app's side. Flags O8 and O13.

42. **2026-10-27, the architect, under O1, O2 and every child that touches the host socket, from O1's branch review (branch p5-o1).** For all of protocol major 3, the lines a pre-O1 peer parses strictly on a protocol-2 link are frozen: the v2 `hello` and `welcome`, `refuse`, `exit`, `req`, `ready`, `res`, `evt`, `documents.open`, `documents.close` and `documents.closed`. `req` keeps a frozen `HostRequestV2Schema` beside the `client`-carrying form O1 M5 added. Any change to a frozen line other than an annotation is breaking. So until major 4 drops the protocol-2 window, a field added to one of them takes one of two routes: either its v2 form splits off and the writer keeps the new field off v2 links, or the change takes a new line kind, which is itself a major bump. Rejected: classifying the retained forms like any other line, which would pass an optional field as minor while a pre-O1 peer refuses it. O2's document channels need neither route. The app-below-2³¹ and endpoint-at-or-above split is a value convention inside `Channel`'s existing `0..2³²−1` range, and the refusal uses `documents.closed`'s existing `host` reason. So `mawsd` enforces it in code, and the split must never become a narrowed schema bound, which would be a breaking change to a frozen line. Flags O2, O3 and O10.

46. **2026-10-27, the architect, under O2 and O16, from O2's Stop-hook probe (branch p5-o2-probe, 5db935c01; the pinned engine 2.1.283 against the stub, on a throwaway devcloud VM since deleted).** The probe's answers: Stop arrives before `result` and carries the exact cron list; an interrupted turn sends no Stop; `-p` has no keepalive, so Decision Log 29's conditional fourth unreadable state is dropped; and a subagent under MAWS's launch is offered no cron tool. One finding extends §2's sources. A durable cron (`CronCreate durable: true`) never appears in any Stop's `session_crons`. It persists in `<cwd>/.claude/scheduled_tasks.json` and fires by itself, including after `--resume` and before any Stop, so reading crons from Stop alone would release a session holding a durable job. The engine host therefore reads that file at each Stop and at a resume's launch, and merges into the work's crons the entries whose `createdBySessionId` matches any engine session id the session has had. Matching the lineage, not only the current id, holds however `--resume` treats ids. A file that exists but can't be read counts as unknown work, and that replaces §2's resume-after-crash state, since the file is what survives a crash. Rejected: marking a session sticky-unknown after a durable `CronCreate`, which can't tell when the job is gone and misses a resume that finds one already in the file. O16 carries the same reading when it re-homes lane B processes. Flags O16.
47. **2026-10-27, the architect, under O2 and O16, correcting Decision Log 46 from O2's executor (the probe's question 4, `spikes/protocol-probe/findings-p5-o2-stop-crons.txt` on p5-o2-probe).** Decision Log 46's clause that the unreadable scheduled-tasks file replaces §2's resume-after-crash state is wrong: the file holds durable jobs only. `--resume` also resurrects the session-only crons the transcript holds (`resume: resurrected 1 session cron task(s)`), and the probe saw one fire, in a turn the engine started, before the launch's first Stop. So a resume that doesn't follow a release counts as unknown work until its first Stop, which lists the resurrected job. A resume after a release stays known-empty: strict release never releases a session whose crons are listed or unknown, and the file read covers its durable jobs. §2 now lists four unreadable states, the original three and the unreadable file; Decision Log 29's conditional keepalive state stays dropped. Rejected: unknown for every resume, which would keep a session resumed after a release with no prompt from ever releasing again until a turn ran. O2 tests a durable job firing before a resume's first Stop in case (m)'s family at M7, with unit cases at M2. Flags O16.
48. **2026-10-27, the architect, under O2, from O2's baseline runs at M1's commit (5527067ed, the mini: 40483272622, 40485317066, 40487334697).** Acceptance 14's memory bound reads each process's physical footprint, the figure macOS charges a process (`ri_phys_footprint`, what Activity Monitor's Memory column shows), not its RSS. Three valid runs of the same idle supervisor read 77,520 to 153,952 KiB of RSS, a 2x spread. RSS drops when macOS compresses an idle process's dirty pages under other jobs' pressure and counts shared clean pages, so it moved with the mini's load, not with the supervisor, and a 1.5x bound would pass or miss on that noise alone. The footprint keeps compressed pages and leaves out shared clean ones, which is the cost the owner gets back when a session is released. O2 re-records the baseline at M1's commit with three more runs, keeps RSS in the output line as a diagnostic, and reports the footprint's spread before deriving the bound if it is still wide. The multipliers of O2's residue 29 stand (1.5 times the footprint, the CPU plus 1.0 s per 600 s). Rejected: keeping RSS and judging a miss by reruns, which spends three runs per miss on noise and leaves the bound unable to catch a real regression of under 2x.
49. **2026-10-27, the architect, under O2 and O16, from O2's executor at M2 (p5-o2 9c8750c83).** Decision Log 47's resumed state, as written, never ends for a resume that gets no turn. A session resumed after an engine-host crash or a major upgrade, and then prompted by nobody, would be held by both the idle release and the memory budget until a cron fired or someone sent a turn. One crash could keep every session it touched for good, which is the cost the laptop's rule exists to give back. So the engine host persists each session's cron knowledge in its state file, beside the `engineSessionIds` lineage M2 adds: the last Stop's session crons and whether a cron tool ran after that Stop. A resume that doesn't follow a release starts known-empty when that record shows no session cron listed and no cron tool since, and unknown until its first Stop otherwise. A listed recurring cron fires, and its Stop ends the unknown. What stays unbounded is a crash in a turn that ran a cron tool and a listed one-shot that never fires, both narrow, and the tracker carries them. Rejected: releasing a resumed unknown after a fixed time, which needs a probe to ground the longest cron interval and would still release a session whose cron is real; and keeping Decision Log 47 as written, which turns a crash into a permanent hold. Flags O16, which re-homes processes with the same record.

54. **2026-10-27, the architect, under O2, from O2's footprint baseline (d14e92991, the same `src/` as M1's 5527067ed; the mini: 40490756731, 40492851056, 40495339208).** The footprint reading holds: three runs spread 1.03x (63,969 to 66,033 KiB) where RSS had spread 2x, and `proc_pid_rusage` agreed with top in each. The baseline is 64,481 KiB and 0.11 s per 600 s. But a combined bound of 1.5 times that leaves `mawsd` about 31 MiB, and `mawsd` runs on the Electron binary as Node (`ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE=1`), as the engine host does, whose bare runtime alone is likely most of that. So the combined bound would judge the daemon shape's second process, which P5 chose, not a regression in either process, and P5 §10's remedy would fire by construction. The memory bound is therefore per process: the engine host within 1.5 times the supervisor's baseline footprint, and `mawsd` within 1.5 times the footprint of the bare runtime it runs on (the same Electron binary as Node with an empty script that stays alive, three runs on the mini under the load rule, the median). The CPU bound stays combined: the baseline plus 1.0 s per 600 s. Rejected: the combined 1.5 times, for the reason above; and a fixed allowance in MiB for `mawsd`, which would be a guess rather than a measurement.

A position was signed twice across a connection replacement. A torn JSONL tail swallowed the next append, in M2's shared helper. The legacy key was not scoped to its org. A departing session's listing was not withdrawn, and forget-before-gone erased its settlement. The host high-water advanced before `wrote` was delivered. A machine signer was resolved by name, not by key. Round two found 2 more: the torn-tail load and cut disagreed, and a lane B listing was withdrawn after an engine-session change. Round three was clean. Its calls, joint with O12 (O12 builds the sminos side as M7b, whose text in PR #235 agrees with M6's frames, fixture path and members-before-uploads order): (1) RL10 gains three frames, since nothing has cut over and no compatibility path is needed. Endpoint to sminos: `spent {project, writer, seq, reason}`, sent only for a verdict no later head can change, so one forged or replayed hub event no longer stalls a member's writer for good; sminos logs each spent position and counts it in `daemon status`. `words?` requires `project`. sminos to endpoint: `restated {}`, sent once after the post-welcome restatement, after which the endpoint withdraws the sessions it did not hear about; it replaces a 15 s timer. (2) The hold rule. `unknown-signer` is held and re-judged at each new head, new certificates or legacy key, capped at 1000 events and one hour each; an event let go comes back from the hub at sminos's next `hello`. `revoked`, `bad-signature` and `malformed` are final. `expired` is final under a key the state retired, and held when the signer is a machine certificate, since a renewal not yet received may cover it. A key that is both retired and certified reads as held. By the remote layer's Decision Log 24, a removed member's keys enter retired or revoked in the same step, so a removed member never reads `unknown-signer`. (3) The endpoint's verdicts map onto the hub's four rejected reasons: replayed to `seen-nonce`, forged to `bad-code`, an own non-echo to `conflict`, a bad shape to `bad-shape`. `revoked` names a device or member id only once the head no longer holds it, so revoking one of a member's devices does not revoke the other. (4) A `words?` whose authority moves while it is being decided gets no answer; sminos re-asks after `WORDS_WAIT`, so the delivery stays owed and a person's words are never downgraded to peer on a race.

The field is published only once every deployed verifier carries M5a's `org-protocol`: the hub, each endpoint, O13's running bundle, and O8's app if landed. 91. **2026-10-28, the architect, under O10, at the landing of the carriers' socket-buffer bound (PR #275, merge 2ffc23dde): Decision Log 77's carrier residue is closed.** reviewer-high ran six rounds (4, 2, 1, 2 and 1 findings, then correct on 5cad3dc05..0413e3894), each finding reproduced with a real-socket or real-relay probe before its fix. Gate: class 1a 40664747575 on the head's tree, based on master's head 872a6c309, so it is the merge gate. Class 1a alone covers it: nothing under `src/engine-host`, `apps/org-protocol`, `apps/desk-hub/src` or desk-web's sources changed; no e2e suite imports `src/endpoint`; desk-web's local cards suite, which does, runs in the unit job through `apps/desk-web/vitest.local.config.ts`; and the wire is unchanged. As built: (1) every frame either carrier sends, pongs included, goes through a send buffer (`src/endpoint/send-buffer.ts`) that feeds the socket one frame at a time; the connection ends at a total ceiling (40 MiB on the hub link, 16 MiB on the direct listener) or at a 30 s stall read from the socket's own writes, and a graceful close drains the buffer first. (2) The direct listener has a 1 MiB soft cap and terminates a connection at its limits; the client resumes from its cursor. (3) The hub link applies back-pressure and does not reconnect: a relayed stream pauses once 1 MiB is buffered until the socket drains, and the 32 MiB per-channel data budget closes only the channel that crosses it. (4) The log-plane uplink runs off RL10's loop in a queue capped at 8 MiB. An overflow drops the upload and marks a hole for that writer; each hole is filled by asking sminos to resend, one ask at a time from a fixed 1 MiB reserve, the ask released after 30 s; a writer refused as final is never asked again, and an upload's writer is held to the hub's name rule. Residue, bounded, no row: a sminos that accepts a resend and never answers delays other writers' asks by at most 30 s in every 60 s, with memory bounded. O10 now has only M10 and M11, both waiting for O2; its xhigh panel runs from p5-desk-track-owner's session once M11 has landed.

Residue accepted, each with its rejected alternative in O3's Decision Log: (1) nothing moves between directories, since O2 made the daemon's directory the profile; (2) one writer, `spine.lock` (O2's `takeLock`), held by whichever process runs the spine, with the index's version as the fence against older builds (rejected: SQLite's own locking, safe for the file but not the state); (3) settings split by a field table into the client's `settings.json` and the machine's `machine-settings.json`, the renderer keeping one merged API and the app-scope OAuth identity still naming `settings.json`; (4) the browser's tables leave the index by a staged transfer, index version 14 being the handoff; (5) the library's derived index moves with the index at O3-D, O4 keeping the library's files; (6) staying code reads the spine through one view; (7) `mawsd` becomes the engine host's one permanent client and the app's attachment an explicit signal, both gated on the engine host's capability, so a host kept across the update keeps O2's semantics; (8) acceptance 14: the spine is never resident while nothing needs it, `mawsd` starting as a cold core, loading the spine on need and releasing or re-executing when quiet (rejected: a child process, kept as the fallback; raising the bound, the owner's call, asked only on a measured miss); (9) one `WIRE_MINOR` bump at each of O3-D and O3-E, a main-to-machine retag being additive. Ruled here: the `[parent-impact]` on OX1 is accepted as an exception, written into OX1: O3-D refuses a pre-O3-A app's hello within the major by its missing capability, and that app's hand-made writes beside a post-D `mawsd` (a new chat's intent, a prompt's history line) are the stated residual (rejected: a protocol major at O3-D, which strands kept sessions across the update). O3-A takes settings version 21 and index migration 14, whoever lands second re-bumping to master's plus one; the new files are `machine-settings.json` (its own version line from 1), `spine.lock` and `browser/browser.sqlite`; O3-C adds the measured group `e2e:daemon-idle-spine`, which CI-opt reviews at its landing. Its findings are accepted. P1: acceptance 2's dataset refresh is O4's, and O3's section says so. P2: the artifacts and journals already live in the daemon's directory, so O3's first task is an ownership migration, as its section now says. P3: from O2 only the supervisor, unit and latency groups run on the daemon shape; O3-B flips the rest, and the Risks row is corrected. P4 and P5 correct O6: a main-to-machine retag is additive, and `machine.json` stays at the profile's root. P6: the survey missed several synchronous readers, which O3-C's one view carries. P7: acceptance 14's bound did not budget for services leaving the app; residue 8 answers it. P8: O3-D owns `hasClient` and `onClientGone` once `mawsd` is the permanent client. P9, a hazard on O2's branch: a `MAWS_E2E` or `MAWS_HARNESS` launch with no `MAWS_USER_DATA` would run on the default profile and could end the owner's `mawsd` and terminals through `leaveDaemon`; O2 closes it before it lands, and O3-B's acceptance keeps the check. P10: configured delivery's OAuth custody depends on the settings file's path, which residue 3 keeps. Also P5 §2's injected services hold only at the composition root: 29 spine files import main's IPC singletons, O3-C's work. The Ordering table now names O3's children: O4 and O5 wait on O3-D (O4's library files also on O3-E), O16 on O3-D, O7 M4 on O3-E and M2, M5 and M6 on O3-D. Contract answers: O7's C1, C2, C3, C5, C6, C7 and C9 and O8's D1 and D3 are delivered by O3-D, C2's and C8's files half by O3-E; C4's start and intents are `machine` acts at O3-D, with the requester-as-owner and its proof going to O7 M4 with O12; C10 is refused as already built (the engine host's store records `createdBy` and `links`); O8's D2 is refused, lane B record changes routing to sminos's member record as lane A's do (Decision Log 45), which O8 M5's re-plan takes; D4 is accepted. Remote panes switch from Decision Log 98's `ListedSession` to records once O3-D lands and O7 offers `session.get`. Correction: acceptance 14's bound is 101,113 KiB, `idleBound` flooring 1.5 times 67,409 by Decision Log 57's rule; O3's document carried 101,114 and is corrected with this entry. The owner's question Q1 (whether Quit still offers to stop sessions after O3-D) is asked; Q2 (the bound, on a measured miss) is asked only if it arises.
102. **2026-10-28, the architect, under O12 and O14, at sminos's follow-ups (sminos 1.27.6 to 1.27.8, doperpowers PRs #427 to #429; the desk track's report).** A correction to Decision Log 100 first: its line that a seq taken above the bridge's own mark keeps a lost `daemon/sends.json` from going below it was wrong. The daemon sweeps a bridge's kept file after a day idle, and a child's failed save leaves the mark behind the mod's, so the bridge mark is no floor; a reused seq against a live mark answers a false `duplicate`, against a swept one it delivers once, so the worst case at 1.27.4 was a false failure, never a double delivery. sminos 1.27.6 (doperpowers #427, 1f4bff14, board #159) makes `sends.json` version 2, `{version, floor, seq}`: `floor` is the highest seq of any dropped entry, a session with no entry starts at the larger of the floor and the bridge mark plus one, pruning runs at most hourly and only for sessions not running, with no bridge socket and nothing in flight, a version 1 file reads as floor 0, and a 1.27.4 daemon refuses a version 2 file rather than reuse a key; reviewer-medium ran two rounds and both reproductions now allocate above the old seq. The tracker row closes. sminos 1.27.7 (#428, board #157) fixes `test-sync.sh`'s setup race, 20 of 20 under load. sminos 1.27.8 (#429, f8718cb0, boards #156 and #158) merges the release queues: file order holds within a queue, a former name goes first on a timestamp tie, and what waits after a hub leave stays queued and is named by `hub leave`; `daemon owed` counts it as having no upstream and exits 1 on it, a behaviour change no O14 gate meets, since none runs `owed` on a machine that has left its hub and devcloud's departure reads only `hub leave`'s exit. Each PR's ci-gate tree equals its merged head's. The cutover's sminos floor stays 1.27.3 (Decision Log 96); mini-ops installs the latest.
103. **2026-10-28, the architect, under O3, O2 and O14, at O3-F's and O3-G's findings (PR #289, merge e949d75d6).** Both spikes ran on one devcloud VM (`dc-o3spikes-b0eb`, deleted at the end), the engine as a dedicated user with no credential against the stub API; the probes gate passed on the findings' tree (run 40692514046), and one reviewer-medium round's six findings were fixed. O3-F: native rewind across `--resume` is not broken on the pinned engine, so RL §12.3's lane B half is answered and O3-D needs no rewind refusal at the launch boundary; O3-D takes a crash-window repair (a rewind's anchor lost to a SIGKILL within about 100 ms of its answer, re-sent at resume with its original target and last-seen cursor). Two residuals outside the launch boundary: the resume chooser's preview shows a rewound turn as the latest after a "Load now" rewind, because that rewind writes its anchor without `lastPrompt` and `LastPromptAnchorSchema` requires it, which lane A shows today and a one-line fix closes; and `currentChain`, used only by the replay sources, ignores rewind anchors between a rewind and the next prompt, a tracker row. O3-G: Node's recursive watch on Linux watches every file, rescans per event and fails silently at exhaustion, so O3-E builds a directory-level watch tree MAWS owns (O3's Decision Log 9). The engine host's store watcher makes the same recursive call over `~/.claude/projects` and already runs wherever O2 puts the engine host on Linux, so O14's VMs carry it from B1: harmless on a fresh VM's few transcripts, it degrades silently as they grow, and it shares O3-E's module (a tracker row until then). O3's Ordering drops O3-G from O3-E's blockers. The run drove the VM partly through the ptc kernel's shell, which the worktree guard does not cover, before switching to the documented `devcloud vm ssh <name> < file` route; nothing ran locally outside its worktree, and later VM briefs name the documented route.
104. **2026-10-28, the architect, under O3, at the landing of O3-F's preview fix (PR #292, merge 8e1ada2a4), and master's unit suite red from #289 until it.** The fix: `LastPromptAnchorSchema`'s `lastPrompt` is optional, since a "Load now" rewind on a resumed session writes its anchor without one. Nothing on the preview path reads `lastPrompt`; the anchor had failed to parse, so `recentTurnsOf`'s leaf and `withResponseGroups`' replay walked past it. Both now cut at its target as the chain follower does, on lanes A and B alike through `session.recentTurns`. On O3-F's pre-release fixture the preview after the rewind and after the release reads T1 where it read T1 and T2, and T1 and T3 once T3 is sent. Tests in `recent-turns.test.ts` and `transcript.test.ts` fail on master and pass with it; the old schema test that asserted the anchor's rejection now accepts the shape the engine recorded. reviewer-medium found nothing; class 1a 40697815990 green on the head's tree f6106b5eb, whose base was master's head. The spike's recorded MAWS-side replay (`p5-o3-rewind-resume-maws.json`) and the probe test that reads it keep the pre-fix preview as the spike's record; a regeneration changes four snapshots and must update that test's assertion with them. A further residual of the same cause: `summarize.ts` takes `lastAssistantText` and `leafUuid` from the last record in file order, so until the next prompt the sidebar digest of a session MAWS does not hold, and `markSeen`'s cursor, name the rewound turn's reply (measured on the fixture, not seen in the app); it joins the `currentChain` tracker row. Master's unit suite was red from #289's merge e949d75d6 until this merge: `replay-fixtures.test.ts` expects every probe journal under the fixture tree by directory, #289 added `fixtures/p5/`, and #289 was landed on a probes-only gate, the architect's error. The PR's second commit adds `p5` to the expectation. No branch in flight had merged master in that window. From now on a probes-only or static-only gate lands only a diff confined to `docs/`; anything else lands on class 1a at least.
105. **2026-10-28, the architect, under O8, O7 and O13, at M1's landing (PR #287, merge 790002644): the app's org session.** Settings' Org section signs a Mac in from a `pair --class native-mac` code: Access's login transfer in the system browser, `/api/me` naming the code's org and member, device keys made at that moment, the chain read from genesis and verified against the code's root pins, the entry posted with the code's MAC and its fingerprint shown. Main holds the chain whole under `<userData>/org/` behind an anchor a damaged file, a rollback, a fork or a stale hub cannot move, and follows the hub through the Registry feed. The production `OrgSession` replaces Decision Log 99's `signedOut()` arm, so O7's links come up in production; an ended Access token sets `hub` to null and keeps the direct carrier, and a hub that stops admitting the member reads `denied` and changes no trust. A profile that never signs in makes no keys, files or requests. New dependency `tweetnacl` 1.0.3; no `WIRE_MINOR`, settings version, `IpcError` code or e2e group. Residue, each with its rejected alternative in O8's Decision Log and report: (1) one org session per run, none in production under `MAWS_DEV_ORG`; (2) up to 120 s of backoff when Access refuses a fresh token, never on the Worker's `not-a-member`; (3) `org.*` is graded `client` on the link surface, and `routes.json` is regenerated after a merge, never hand-merged; (4) `ORG_REFUSALS` gains `directory` and the feed gains `onClose`; (5) a registration with no readable `access.json` reads signed out; (6) the chain is re-read from a recovery's `fromVersion`, or from genesis when the served head is below the held one; (7) the held view as built (files, `certificatesChanged`, `highWater()`, rollback and fork judged only when nothing was adopted); (8) the transfer's box key comes from WebCrypto, and cloudflared's metadata `iat` check is not done until the staging sitting shows whether Access sets `iat`; (9) one serialized commit queue for every durable write and trust change; (10) hub answers sanitized at the boundary, malformed certificate items dropped. Evidence: reviewer-high two rounds (3 P1 and 5 P2 fixed, each with a failing-first test; the second round clean); the sign-in against desk-hub's Worker in Miniflare with O13's real `pair` (7 passed); final gate 40683839806 on the head's tree: class 1a, `unit:darwin`, `e2e:default` and 29 of 32 hosted groups green; `e2e:c7` (`composer.spec.ts:183`) green on rerun 40699088283; `e2e:design-systems` (`design-systems.spec.ts:140`, the preview iframe's context destroyed after the accent edit) reaches nothing #287 changes; `packaged` 199 passed with the one known red `layout-transfer.spec.ts:541`, which O2's branch skips, so every packaged spec that launches main with `tweetnacl` bundled passed. Master had moved in code only under `.github`, the fixtures and `transcript.ts`, none imported by #287. It conflicts with O2 in `src/main/index.ts` and `src/shared/ipc/grades.ts`; it landed first so that O2's final set on its merge covers both, one hosted set instead of two. Owed: the owner's staging sitting (the fingerprint on a real Access login, `/api/me` from the app, a relaunch with no browser trip), folded after O13 M7's in the desk track's sitting.
106. **2026-10-28, the architect, under O14, at the landing of the desk track's PR #291 (merge 258b13632): `provision.test.ts`'s pool flake found and fixed.** The local hub never died on its own: `apps/desk-hub/local/run.ts` threw. Node's HTTP server takes its own `error` listener off a socket before it emits `upgrade`, and the stand-in's proxy (`apps/desk-hub/local/proxy.ts`) added one only once the hub had upgraded. In the retire case that reconciles a lost DELETE answer, the restarted endpoint's hub-link upgrade is refused 401 and `HubLink.stop()` resets the socket while the refusal is unread, so the reset raised an unhandled ECONNRESET and ended the process: the next case failed ECONNRESET and the six after it ECONNREFUSED, both pool sightings' signature (Decision Logs 95 and 102). Reproduced locally with instrumentation, 1 run in 20 under load on the unfixed tree. The proxy's upgrade handler now owns the socket's errors from the start, and a client gone mid-handshake closes the hub's side; `apps/desk-hub/test/proxy.test.ts` covers resets before the answer, after a refusal, and before and after the hub upgraded; `test/endpoint/local-hub.ts` prints the hub's output tail when it exits unasked. No retry, no test case changed. Evidence: 20 local runs of unit 4/4's file set at 2753c929c (provision and proxy 20 of 20, no hub deaths); class 1a 40694837226 there, and on the head's tree after merging master, class 1a 40703392641 and hosted `e2e:desk-web-hub` 40704168662 (the group ci-gate's coverage note named, since its specs reach `local-hub.ts`); reviewer-medium no material findings; master had moved since only in #293's scripts. The tracker row closes. A local observation, not from CI: on the loaded MacBook `src/main/files/service.test.ts`'s listing cap case, which writes 5,001 files inside the 5 s default timeout, timed out in 8 of the 20 runs; never seen on the pool, so no row.
107. **2026-10-28, the architect, under O2, O3, O10 and O14, at O2's landing (PR #296, merge 7a5893b2c): `mawsd`, the per-user daemon.** `mawsd` is a per-profile daemon outside Electron that owns the engine host and the pty host: on macOS the default profile's LaunchAgent runs it, which a packaged app installs or repairs; on Linux a systemd user unit with lingering, installed by `devenv up` into a content-named release with its own Node 24.21.0; any other profile starts a private one. Main reaches the two hosts and `daemon.*` through three relayed sockets. Release is strict by interest: a session with no tab and no keep mark is released after the idle time even with the app open, and never while it holds crons (Stop's and the durable `scheduled_tasks.json`), background tasks or work it cannot read. Terminals outlive the app. Settings version 20 makes the daemon shape the default, with embedded as the owner's fallback; the wire is minor 5 (master's 242 manifest entries plus O2's 18). A test or harness launch on the default profile exits non-zero before any host work (O3's planner P9). Acceptance 14's bound: Decision Log 57's fixed 96,721 KiB is superseded. After the landing merges the first daemon run read 101,411 KiB against it, `mawsd` having grown to about 31 MiB with O10's endpoint wiring and the engine host spreading 66 to 70 MiB run to run, so the bound is re-derived on the landing tree as 1.5 times the detached supervisor measured there, re-measured whenever a landing misses: 65,729, 70,881 and 67,409 KiB in CI-opt's quiet window, median 67,409, so 101,113 KiB and 1.11 s. The daemon shape read 97,683 and 99,219 KiB there, a third run invalid on load 7.6 from ci-kit's VM lane (Gate offload Decision Log 137): within, with about 1.9% to spare, and the local-latency bench's p50 at 25.9 of 26.45 ms. Later merges changed no code under `src/mawsd`, `src/engine-host` or `src/pty-host`. The lever is `mawsd`'s eager start-up graph, and O3 budgets against the bound before it adds to either process. Gates on the code head's tree 892d99838: class 1a 40703662055; hosted 40704450189, 36 of 37 with desk-web-hub and cross-build (3.5 against 3.4), `connectors-direct.spec.ts:591` green on rerun 40710872036; the mini's packaged, `e2e:default` and `unit:darwin` 40704479546. Master then moved in #291, #293 and #294, which share `gate.yml` and `gate-plan.test.mjs` with O2 and the endpoint's local hub with its `src/endpoint` changes, so the merge tree was gated as well: class 1a 40714473721 and hosted desk-web-hub, supervisor-daemon and daemon-unit 40714568127. Three merge-resolution reviews and the high-rung branch review were Ready; CI-opt reviewed the `.github` changes (Gate offload Decision Log 141). Residue in the tracker: the idle margin, `daemon.ts`'s size, three host supervision gaps, two Linux pack items, the ad hoc signature's TCC prompts, and unit flakes (`cli-prompt.test.ts`'s ^C case, `ipc.test.ts:1140`). The devcloud VMs `dc-o2probe-ac5c` and `dc-m8accept-dcef` may linger in the desk's Machines list, the desk track's to drop. With O2 in: O3-A and O3-B dispatch; O10 M10 and M11, O14 M1's `src/mawsd` half and B1 are unblocked for the desk track; CI-opt's packaged admission runs its final trials on a tree that carries the daemon shape's packaged check.

113. **2026-10-28, the architect, under O8, O7 and O9, at M2's landing (PR #295, merge cfa0f0e7b): other machines' sessions as rows.** M2 draws the sessions an RL2 machine lists (RL6 listings, verified against the org state) as rows in the sidebar, cached across launches, with a per-lane opener registry that O7 and O9 fill (a click with no opener selects only). Its residue: (11) one rows queue applies every event, and the cache's read comes first, so nothing is judged before the persisted marks are held; rejected: judging live records while the cache loads. (13) A held record that fails verification again leaves the rows and waits, its mark kept; rejected: dropping the mark with the row. (14) A mark's delegation comes only from a certificate `verifyMachineCert` accepts, and verified certificates that disagree record null, which keeps the mark until the key itself ends; rejected: the first certificate matching machine and key, and asking `verifyListing` for its certificate (a change to `org-protocol`). (15) Delegated rows aren't re-verified on the clock, since O7's roster drops a machine at its certificate's expiry; rejected: a rows clock beside the roster's. (16) Listed rows are their own sidebar tree-node kind with no menu, merged by `issued` after the records the sort puts first, and a click with no opener selects the row only; rejected: ranking listed rows into the attention tiers, a union `sessions` touching every reader, and a persisted selection. (17) A covered machine is settled once, when it newly becomes covered, and skips the `engine.sessions` re-read at host ready; rejected: settling at every rows event, which closes tabs as listings churn. 115. **2026-10-28, the architect, under O10 and O7, at the landing of the desk track's PR #301 (merge 683ac3e54): `ws` in main's bundle stays on its JavaScript paths.** O10 M10's scene found that `ws`, bundled into main (O7's `MachineLink`) and into `mawsd` (the endpoint's hub link), threw `bufferUtil$1.mask is not a function` on every masked client frame of 48 bytes or more: its optional native helpers `bufferutil` and `utf-8-validate` are installed nowhere, the bundler stands an empty object in for each, and `ws`'s `try { require(...) }` takes the stand-in as present. 127. **2026-10-28, the architect, under O10 and O8, at the landing of the desk track's PR #317 (merge ea90b9e99): the endpoint follows a recovery whose first state is a sibling of its head.** Decision Log 126's residue for the endpoint is closed. `src/endpoint/org-sync.ts` changes only which states a sync reads, while `#adopt` and `#rewind` verify as before: every state a sync reads goes into one pool that `#adopt` judges whole; when a recovery transition qualifies, half of `MAX_PAGES` goes to it, bases are probed newest first at two pages each, and the whole stays within `MAX_PAGES`; a bare sibling still reads as a fork, with the head, the status and `org.json` byte-identical. Proof: `test/endpoint/org-recovery.local.test.ts` on desk-hub's Worker in Miniflare, where the recovery is followed (the case fails on master) and the bare sibling stays a fork, and `org-sync.test.ts` 24 of 24. reviewer-high took four rounds, each of the first three finding P2s fixed with a test that failed first (a re-read that replaced the forward read and stalled at `MAX_PAGES`, per-probe allowances that multiplied against a hostile hub, a forward read that starved the recovery and a probe-keyed map that crashed), the fourth finding it correct. CI-opt reviewed the gate's half. Readings on d0802b29d, KiB of physical footprint: the A-B-A run 40778748076 read A 65537, B 95907 (`mawsd` 29409, the engine host 66497) and A' 66609, so B is x1.452 of the A mean, the engine host in B reads +424 KiB against its own A mean, and the daemon shape's whole cost is `mawsd`'s. That ratio is acceptance 14's own measure taken without the drift between jobs that put master's tree over 101113 at O10 M10, so it is the number to hold when the bound's scene changes; its headroom is about 3%. The plain baselines: 40791210494 read 66737 at load 2.4, and 40795119204 66097 at 3.7. Two were void on load and are cited only: 40789442114 (load 9.5, Apple Intelligence's app-content indexers after the reboot, `corespotlightd` at 121% at 23:58:36Z beside `spotlightknowledged`, with Spotlight's file indexing off on every volume and no co-tenant job) and 40793346639 (load 5.4, on #319's head 178eb02d8, whose supervisor and host chunks differ from d0802b29d's by 48 lines of O8 M3's org schemas). `DAEMON_IDLE_BASELINE` is now commit d0802b29d, runs 40778748076 (the A mean, 66073), 40791210494 and 40795119204: a median of 66097 KiB and 0.12 s, so the bound is 99145 KiB and 1.12 s; O2's record stays beside it as `DAEMON_IDLE_BASELINE_O2`. What spoils a measured window on the mini, found tonight: a Screen Sharing session to it makes the settle refuse on 'remote' until it closes (CI-opt closed one at 22:30:50Z); Apple Intelligence's indexers run in bursts after a boot, which the load rule catches, and the owner chose to turn Apple Intelligence off on the mini; and Activity Monitor, open on its desktop since the boot, costs about 5% CPU in every window alike.
