129. **2026-10-29, the architect, under O8, at M5's landing (PR #323, merge 0db3870a0): rows name their owners, and a People toggle shows others' sessions where there are any.** M5 (O8's Interfaces §L) puts owners on the sidebar. A project or session row whose owners include someone besides you reads them as a label of two entries at most, 'you' among them ('you, Ana +1'), with names derived from the held head's member emails by `memberName` (`src/shared/org/member-name.ts`: the local part, capitalised, widened to the whole email where two collide); an id the head no longer lists reads 'former member'. `OrgRows` now carries `members` and the signed-in `member`, and a project counts others' sessions as '+N others'. The People toggle, on project and machine rows, hides or shows others' sessions; hidden rows add no activity, so what you can't see doesn't reorder your list, and the choice persists under `sidebar.people`. Two decisions changed in the build. The toggle shows only where it can act, when the row has others' sessions or while it is on: the first build showed it on every row, a dead control for anyone with no org, and its hover action took the line-center click of `e2e/shell.spec.ts:50` at the 200px floor in the final gate (run 40786951312); the alternative, every org-keyed row, still offered a toggle with nothing behind it. And a toggle made before the stored set is read records the state the person saw it reach and is merged key by key when the read lands: reviewer-medium showed the first build's replay as a flip turned a stored-on project off and saved it (fixed at 854178726 with a test that failed first). O8's Decision Log has the other points its build settled. Three test-only fixes rode with it: `OrgSection.test.tsx` sets its own frozen clock, closing Decision Log 126's tracker row; the org-session tests' teardown retries a non-empty `rm`; and the rekey crash test's set-up now waits for the queued anchor write, a race a 30 ms probe reproduced exactly, while the production path writes the intent inside the queue and has no such race. The last two trace to one cause, `close()` not awaiting the commit queue's tail (`org-session.ts:1527`), harmless in production since `close()` runs only at quit; a tracker row. Gates: the final set on 7e58d6984, run 40794571273 (class 1a pool, every hosted Electron group, `e2e:default`, `e2e:discover`, `unit:darwin`), green with one flaky line, `browser-cua.spec.ts:483`, the known orphaned-connector signature on a path M5 does not change; the head differs from that tree only in docs. Master had moved since M5's base in `src/endpoint`, desk-hub, the gate scripts and the daemon-idle support, and `test/org/signer.local.test.ts`, which master changed, imports M5's `src/shared/ipc/schema/org.ts`, so class 1a and `packaged` ran on the merge tree, run 40803576379, green. Residue: the tracker rows below; others' sessions still reach Needs you and 'Mark all read', which is attention across machines, O7 M6's and O3's; `stores/summary.ts`'s `load()` has no production caller, so `summary.sections` is never read back, a debt older than O8. M4 (the refused attempt and the Org section's split) is next, its plan re-made first since it stood advisory. This entry also records #320 (merge d56621550): the endpoint's `DirectListener` deduplicated its address report against the last set it reported and `stop()` never reset that set, so a re-provision that rebound the same addresses kept the `bound:false` that unenroll had written, found at O14 B1's step 6; `stop()` now clears it inside the serialized teardown, with tests that failed first in `direct-listener.test.ts` and in `provision.test.ts`'s delegated unenroll scene; class 1a and hosted `e2e:desk-web-hub` 40801804781, reviewer-low correct. And #321 (merge 816da78bc): CI-opt's hosted flake row rewritten by signature under gate-offload Decision Log 140 (c) and (d).
130. **2026-10-29, the architect, under O8, at the landing of M4's re-plan (PR #328, merge 0f5e4ee87): the Org page is three milestones, because the app would be the first implementation of member and grant administration, not a port.** O8's research on master found no admin verb anywhere that adds or removes a member, changes a role or an email, renews a delegation or issues a grant; nothing signs a grant outside test vectors. Grants are not in `OrgState` but separate `Signed<Grant>` records held by the hosting machine's ledger and delivered through `endpoint.grant`, so a re-enrolled machine refuses every old grant and the app needs its own record of the grants it issued. The app's `StateSigner` signs whole states only, so pairing your own device needs `endorseDevice` under the step-up key and a grant needs `signRecord`, both new signing paths behind Touch ID. The architect's rulings, as re-planned: M4a, executable, carries Q1's four groups, the read-only views, member edits (add, remove, role, email; Q1's People group has 'Add person', so members stay in M4a), device edits (pairing from the app with your own devices endorsed in one Touch ID, another member's first device through H1, removal, root add), each through a preview and change pair bound to a digest of what was shown, `refused.attempt`, and `OrgSection.tsx` split by group; M4b (machines and grants) and M4c (delegations) stay advisory with these facts written in. No app-side copies kept in step by parity tests: `successorProblems`, one pure pre-sign function for ordinary successors, joins `apps/org-protocol` beside `verifySuccessor`, which is unchanged, and both admin's `amend` and the app run it; the pairing judges move there too, as a mechanical first commit the desk track reviews, with its three conditions in M4a's steps (admin from the deploy checkout loads the moved code, checked read-only and by a direct unbundled import since `deploy/bin.ts` bundles admin; a clean wrangler dry-run when M4b moves `encodeEnrollmentCodeV2`; #261 and the move merge, never rebase). `close()` fences and joins every writer under `org/`, which retires the two test workarounds and closes Decision Log 129's tracker row. One correction to the architect's wording, adopted: a member's later device can be paired by that member only if the member signs, since only a root signs a state; a non-signing member's later device has no route until the countersign flow (RL §5.2, deferred by Decision Log 34), and the page says so where the action would be. The hub rebuilds its members table from each accepted head, so 'Add person' and then that person's first device through H1 works end to end, which M4a's local test proves with a second identity. Buildability: two doperpowers:adversarial-reviewer rounds. The first found 1 P1 (pairing approval not bound to the fingerprint compared; the candidate is now frozen when found and approved by its generation and digest, and a same-id repost with other keys ends the pairing), 5 P2 and 1 P3, all adopted; the second confirmed them closed and found two more, adopted as the reviewer prescribed (liveness checked after Touch ID's confirmation, since `apply` runs before it, and `close()` also joining the held view's own adoption queue). Both rounds kept members in M4a.

Residue, each with the alternative it rejected: (1) M9 and M10, remote lane A, are next, every dependency landed; (2) `/usage`, `/config` and `/permissions` for a lane A session wait on the bridge's MW1 reads and show unavailable; (3) a local background shell's output tail stays remote-only (rejected: main reading the output file without sminos's output-answer path rules); (4) a typed `/model x` or `/effort x` on a driven session opens the selector rather than applying (rejected for now: the bridge's `model` op, which replaces model and effort together); (5) a media resend after refused words re-uploads the files (rejected: trimming the draft from outside the editor); (6) `e2e:lane-a`'s hosted admission is CI-opt's, the group staying on the mini until then; (7) `test/fixtures/lane-a/dx4-jobs.json` copies doperpowers' `sminos/tests/dx4-jobs.json` with no re-sync rule, a tracker row (rejected: reading it across repositories at test time); (8) the local MCP read lists only connected servers, so disabled ones need a sminos read (rejected: reading the engine's MCP configuration from main); (9) call sites that still call lane B's commands directly are listed in the PR (rejected: moving them all behind the port in M4). Two lessons from its retrospective the architect keeps for every child: custody, who drives a session at this moment, needs one source from the first milestone that has two lanes (here the controls port's `turn()`, which ended the landing review's defects); and a renderer module another app imports needs that app's group in the class 1a of every change to it, since `e2e:desk-web` sat red for two milestones that no gate ran it.

133. **2026-10-29, the architect, under O2, at the landing of the desk track's PR #324 (merge ce9d875cf): MAWS's three long-lived Node processes run one memory-reducing collection at each entry into idle, which puts the daemon shape 6.6 to 7.8% under acceptance 14's bound on master's own tree and settles Decision Log 124's owed (2).** Why: each process grows V8's young generation to 16 MiB in its start and, when V8 shrinks it, keeps those pages for a run-dependent share of runs, which no later collection returns; collected at the first idle entry, while the pages are still the heap's, `mawsd` held 27.8 to 29.8 MB and the engine host 64.9 to 67.1 MB in the daemon scene on the MacBook, against 29.2 to 40.9 and 65.5 to 88.6 plain. The lever: `mawsd` (`DaemonClocks.quiet`), the engine host (`Supervision.quiet`) and the pty host (`PtyTable.quiet`) each tell the collector their quiet at every reading, and the collector asks again synchronously at the moment it collects, so a late busy signal can only delay a collection; an entry whose heap grew under 1 MiB collects nothing unless a dropped holding forces it; the gc extension is made in one fresh context with `--expose-gc` on only for that moment, so no global `gc` exists; each collection logs its duration and heap; the busy-source tables pin the class, not the instance (17 rows on the engine host, 4 on `mawsd`, 2 on the pty host). The pause measured 26 to 123 ms over 23 MacBook runs up to 30 long replayed sessions, driven by live small objects (about 25 ms plus 0.4 ms per MiB of object-dense live heap), with one unexplained 104 to 108 ms outlier at about 96 MiB used; the architect ruled no design change and no heap-size guard, since the cost is a one-time delay to the next request after an idle entry and a guard would take the reclaim from the heaviest users, and no row unless real use logs a pause past 100 ms. Rejected: a 1 MiB semi-space cap, the same footprint for a flag at every spawn (`--js-flags` in utility processes and the LaunchAgent's argv); `--semi-space-growth-factor=1` set in-process, which works only because V8 reads it at heap setup. The proof ran on the mini inside CI-opt's measured hold and was judged by the burst-tolerant load rule the architect ruled at 03:25Z, before any of these runs: a window is void only when it peaks at 5 or more and two adjacent 30 s intervals of the deconvolved load reach 4.7. Its verdicts came from the shipped `invalidReasons` and `loadPair` at a2fc850c6 of the load-rule branch, replayed on each run's recorded samples, never by hand, and every e2e:daemon-idle run of the series that the rule finds valid counts. Four runs on the merged head ad096787f: 92,579, 92,083, 91,891 and 91,395 KiB (40814726160, 40820891730, 40820904200, 40822960789), each at most 94,188, 5% under 99,145, the smallest margin 6.62%; `mawsd` 28,193 to 28,817, 624 KiB; CPU at most 0.13 s against 1.12 s. Two of them were void under the peak rule on silent thread wakes in which no process showed CPU. The A-B-A on the head (40816219223) was void under both rules on XProtect Remediator's scans; as data it read x1.454 against x1.452 before the lever, so the lever lowers the engine host, which both shapes run, and the margin comes from there rather than from the daemon shape's own cost. Master's additions since the lever's own tree 781b88cb0 (lane A's ipc tables, the people-sidebar layout schema and `OrgMemberSchema` in the host chunks, `mawsd`'s re-split index chunk) are a measured +0.8 MiB in the engine host's mean over six valid windows on each tree; the architect's earlier condition that the head read within 1 MiB of 781b88cb0 was retired once the landing tree carried its own series. The enrolled increment on the same head, run 40826529043, read +5,456 KiB (unenrolled 90,643, enrolled 96,099, `mawsd` +5,904 and the engine host -448), inside Decision Log 114's 8,192 KiB budget and valid by the same rule (void under the peak rule on one sample of 5.5), the first valid reading on a tree with the collection (the earlier valid one, 40735795915 at +5,728, predated it, and 40745657584's +9,184 was `mawsd`'s young-generation spread). One record stated as it was: O2 landed (Decision Log 107) on a series in which 40634705341, at 103,683 KiB, sat above the then-bound 101,113; void as a burst under the peak rule, it is a valid miss under the burst-tolerant rule, so O2's daemon shape was over its bound in one valid reading. This PR is what puts master inside 99,145; no earlier record is restated. Gates on the head's tree: class 1a with the supervisor, supervisor-daemon, daemon-unit, terminal, widgets and desk-web-hub groups (40812803836, five of them placed on the EC2 Mac by the overflow gate-offload Decision Log 145 meant only for unlaned calls); the other hosted groups, browser-comment, unit:darwin and packaged (40825261353); e2e:default on the mini (40831490156). Master had moved since the PR's base by #329's presentations export, #335's gate scripts and #334's lane A fixes, whose engine host changes (`fs/backfill.ts`, `fs/summarize.ts`, `shared/wire/transcript.ts`) no file or test of this PR imports, so the merge commit was not gated again. The tracker row for acceptance 14's headroom closes, and a row holds the burst-tolerant rule until its own PR lands it on master.
134. **2026-10-29, the architect, under O8 and the desk track, at the landing of the desk track's PR #338 (merge 291491219): the hub refuses a machine key that another ref already holds, whatever route the key came by, and the org has one member email grammar, strict where an email is written; the PR also lands O8 M4a's first two tasks, which it was built on.** `POST /machine/key` answers `409 key-held` when the posted key is held under another ref by any holder in the head's state (machines, devices, delegations, the recovery key), by any key the head holds retired or revoked, the machine's own included, or by another enrollment's posted key; the architect ruled that kept certificates count in both directions, since otherwise posting order would decide which ref a key speaks for and `machineSigners` and `machineOwners` could name different owners for one key, so `/machine/key` refuses a key a kept certificate holds under another ref and `storeCert` refuses a certificate whose key the head or another enrollment holds under another ref (mawsd's provision then exits on `CertRefused`, `409 key-held`); each direction has a case that fails without it. remote-architecture agreed and needed no change to the trust model: everything a machine key signs binds its own ref inside the signature, so the hub's refusal is a courtesy that verifiers need not mirror. `isMemberEmail` (org-protocol `names.ts`) is the one grammar, the address as Access names one; `successorProblems` (`email-grammar`) and the admin's genesis hold writers to it, readers stay tolerant (`isOrgState`, `verifySuccessor`, the hub's lower-cased resolve), and the app's `hub-client` reads `/api/me`'s email only in that grammar: the architect's earlier word that this reader was tolerant was wrong about the code, and it stays strict on purpose, so that a hostile hub can't reflect the bearer token as an email, with that reason in the code; a later grammar change checks the stored emails first. The regular expression is the one the hub client already had, so the app's behaviour does not change. Carried in from O8 M4a, as sequenced: Task 1, the pairing judges moved from `deploy/admin.ts` into org-protocol (336af704b), and Task 2, `successorProblems` and `stateProblems` with diagnostics bounded before authentication and a comment that their full mode is not for unauthenticated input (5935f62a5, 592211929, 37ce2077d), both reviewed clean under O8 and approved by the desk track. O8 M4a, landing second, switches `edits.ts` to `isMemberEmail`, adds `email-grammar` to its plan's pre-sign codes, and takes this PR's change to its 'twin posts m1's key' case, now a key another signer lists because the hub refuses the twin. Gates: class 1a with hosted `e2e:desk-web-hub` and `e2e:desk-web` on the head's tree (40835319298, no flaky line; 40834069677 before master's merge); reviewer-high clean on 37ce2077d..a650031ce and on the certificate delta. The hub change takes effect only with a production deploy of `desk-hub`, which is the owner's. Residue in the tracker: staging's `OrgAdmin` composes without `successorProblems`, and no case revokes an enrollment during `keepMachineKey`'s key-id await.
135. **2026-10-29, the architect, under O9 and the desk track, at the landing of the desk track's PR #339 (merge 2505e72e3, with sminos 1.27.12, doperpowers #442 at 28f5a49f7): DX4's `step-end` carries the step's end time `t` and its `model` and `effort`, each optional and additive, so that a remote lane A session gets the two fixes of O9's sitting that read them.** O9's remote half found that Stop's partial text places a cut reply by its step-end's `t` (`placeCuts`), and that the model chip reads the latest main-loop step's model and effort, while DX4's `step-end` carried only `{turnId, agentId, step}` and DX4 has no step start; a time stamped on the receiving Mac is the wrong clock and is wrong under replay. The architect asked the desk track for the fields, ahead of B2's sminos update on the mini. Semantics: `t` is the DX5 stamp of the event that ended the step (its step end, or its turn's completion for a step cut off by it); `model` and `effort` come from the step's DX5 start with the main loop's bridge override applied, `effort: null` passed through when the request named none; a field the plane doesn't know is absent (a watch that joins mid-step has the snapshot's `model` and no `effort`); the bridge holds a step's end up to 1 s while its blocks get rows, so `t` can trail the request's finish by up to 1 s, as local lane A sees. MAWS's side: `StepEnd` in `apps/desk-hub/src/protocol/session.ts`, the fake machine and the DX4 test reader pass the fields through, and lane A's overlay reads `t` as optional. O9's M9 builds the fallbacks (a remote cut stays unplaced; the chip shows the override, else `read status`'s model) and reads the fields whenever present, with cases for a step-end with and without them, so a sminos carrying them needs no MAWS change. The mini ran sminos 1.27.11 at B2's step 1, before #442; the desk track has mini-ops update it to 1.27.12 before O9's M10 sitting. Gates: class 1a with hosted `e2e:desk-web-hub` and `e2e:desk-web` on the head's tree (40835962872, no flaky line); reviewer-medium found nothing in either repository; master had moved since the head's base by #324, #337 and #338, none of whose files this PR touches, and the change is additive, so the merge commit was not gated again.
136. **2026-10-29, the architect, under O14 and O2, at the landing of PR #343 (merge 5d31f621f, from the desk track): `mawsd endpoint attest-history` waits up to an hour, and one run goes at a time.** B2's step 9 on the Mac mini (about 390 DX1 writers) never got a verdict: the command reached the daemon as `daemon.endpoint` under the client's default 10 s deadline (`REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS`), while it reads every inventory writer's events from the hub page by page, so the CLI gave up and the daemon ran on with its outcome unseen. The session running B2's steps then retried on the timeout: two attest runs, overlapping, and about 24 checks, one about every 25 s, between 08:45 and 08:56Z, each a full read of the production hub's history beside the ones still running. The hub answered normally after it (08:57Z), the mini's `mawsd` was idle by 08:57Z, and its log shows no hub error; nobody read the hub's own logs or Durable Object state, which only the owner reads (wrangler against production), so that check of 08:45 to 08:57Z is on the owner's list. The two overlapping attest runs may have posted the same writers twice; the first `--check` after this fix shows whether the stored attestations verify. The fix: `attest-history`, attesting and `--check` alike, sends `daemon.endpoint` with a 60 min deadline (`ATTEST_HISTORY_TIMEOUT_MS` in `src/mawsd/cli.ts`), every other endpoint command keeping 10 s and the shared default unchanged; a fixed deadline rather than `--wait`, since the module waits only on its own hub reads, each bounded at 30 s by the hub client. The endpoint module keeps the run in progress per endpoint and refuses a second one, check or attest, naming the run and its start, rather than joining it (a second caller's names, project or output may differ, and a check beside an attest would judge records still being posted); each run logs its start and its outcome in `mawsd`'s log and holds `mawsd` against its idle exit until it ends. The tests fail first with the fix stashed: through the real `machine.sock`, attest-history is answered past 10 s while `status` beside it still gives up at 10 s; against a stand-in hub holding its first read, a second check, an attest and a narrowed check are refused without reading the hub, and a run goes ahead after a finished or failed one. The rule this leaves for any long endpoint command, which the desk track's O14 record writes into B3's and B4's runbook text: one run, the caller waits for the command's own deadline, never a retry on a timeout, then one `--check`. Gates: class 1a pool and hosted `e2e:desk-web-hub` (39 passed), run 40847959810 on eb72b2ced, its tree the head's, no flaky line; the head sat on master c6c523547, unmoved. reviewer-medium found nothing material (its lens the hold and its release paths: the hold affects only the idle exit and quiet collection, never quit, restart or status).

O3-D now waits only on O3-C.
138. **2026-10-29, the architect, under O10 and O14, at the landing of PR #345 (merge a05b85331, from the desk track): a lane A question raised after its project opened reaches the hub's presence and the phone.** B2's step 7 (the phone card test, the Mac mini on the RL10 plane) failed: no pending request from any mini session reached the hub, so the Cards view showed nothing waiting and the endpoint kept no `notified.json`. The endpoint's Notifier counted only a project's whole pending set, which sminos sends when it opens the project (after every RL10 welcome), while each question raised afterwards, and each answer, travels as a single sub-less `raised` or `settled` session message that the link handed only to PlaneA; so no raise after a project opened ever reached `notify waiting`. Now the Notifier keeps each project's lane A requests by id for each session: a whole set replaces what was held for its project, a sub-less `raised` adds its one request and a `settled` naming it takes it away (a raise already held, or a settlement of a request not held, moves nothing), and every change of a session's count is told to the hub once as before (`src/endpoint/notify.ts`, `src/endpoint/rl10/link.ts`). Tests fail first without the fix: the records suite drives raises and settlements between sets, and the RL10 conversation fixture gains the raise-and-decline exchange (`test/endpoint/rl10/conversation.json`; sminos keeps a copy of that fixture, which the desk track refreshes in doperpowers after this landing). On desk-hub-staging a blocking ask set `notified.json` to pending 1 and the hub's presence to waiting 1, both cleared on decline; the control run without the fix stayed at `{}` after 90 s. Gates: class 1a pool and hosted `e2e:desk-web-hub` on the daemon-shape harness (39 passed), run 40852192868 on a9796a478 (the fix with master bd6b4b097 merged), its tree the head's, no flaky line. reviewer-high found no material defect; its one P2 is the sminos copy above. The mini's app is rebuilt from master with this and #343 for B2's steps 7 and 9, outside the measured series' hold.
139. **2026-10-29, the architect, under O2, at the landing of the desk track's PR #340 (merge b22b7e419): the measured idle windows void on sustained load, not on a burst (Rule B, as Decision Log 133 ruled it).** `test/support/daemon-idle.ts` rebuilds each 30 s interval's own load from the 1-minute series (the exact inverse of its 60 s exponential average for a load constant over the interval), takes the window's 30 s pair as the highest load two adjacent intervals both held, and voids a window on load only when its peak sample reached 5 and its 30 s pair reached 4.7; the pair only guards the peak, so every window valid before stays valid, which a seeded spread of 4,003 series pins, and the void reason names both numbers. Every line that prints a load prints the pair beside the peak, and each sample records the machine's CPU times, busy share and top process (a process table read in the test runner, outside the measured set). The boundary windows are unit fixtures copied literally from their runs' recorded series: the sustained-indexer baselines stay void, and four burst-only windows (40805962574, 40809164967, 40502284016, 40634705341) now pass. This supersedes the validity sentence of the O2 plan's M1 acceptance (`docs/doperpowers/plans/2026-10-27-p5-o2-mawsd.md`, the 1-minute load under 5 at every sample), which now points here. Proof: one e2e:daemon-idle run on the mini on the merged head e78150ab3 inside CI-opt's measured hold, run 40852439950, green: peak 3.8 (the burst sampler's 5 s peak 3.88, none at 5), footprint 91,987 KiB (median of 21) against the bound 99,145, a 7.22% margin (`mawsd` 28,625, `maws-supervisor` 63,361), CPU 0.12 s over 600 s against 1.12; inside every range of #324's landing set (91,395 to 92,579 KiB, `mawsd` 28,193 to 28,817, CPU 0.11 to 0.13), so the new per-sample table left the measured set unchanged. The table's own cost on the mini is unread, since a valid run uploads no `daemon-idle.json`; the MacBook's reading is about 60 ms of CPU per table, once every 30 s, and the architect took the unchanged measured set as enough. Class 1a pool 40851347615 on e78150ab3, its tree the head's; the master moves after it (#345 under src/endpoint, and records) touch nothing the branch reaches. 144. **2026-10-29, the architect, under O9, at the landing of PR #355 (merge 01d07b75e): lane A shows the permission mode a terminal session reports, from sminos 1.27.14 on.** sminos 1.27.14 (doperpowers #445, board #160) carries `permissionMode`, `permissionModeAt` and `permissionModeFrom` on the bridge's heartbeat and `read status`. `src/shared/lane-a/mode.ts` parses them, a report counting only when all three are present, well typed and the word is one of the engine's six modes, and guards the bridge's version; `LaneAState.mode` carries the reading. Locally the driver reads it from the state stream's heartbeat (`src/services/lane-a/state.ts`, `driver.ts`); remotely `src/main/lane-a/remote-state.ts` reads it from the status read the adapter already makes and holds it to the launch it was read under, so a session resumed in a new process under the same engine id never shows the previous process's mode (reviewer-medium's one P2, fixed in 7839ead63 with unit and integration cases that fail without it, re-reviewed clean). The Mode chip shows the engine's title for the mode (`default` is 'Manual'), its hover gives the report's age refreshed every 30 s, and its menu checks the mode with every choice still closed, since MAWS sets no mode on a terminal session; Session info shows the mode with its age, and a remote session's Session info gains a Permissions group holding the Mode row; 'Not reported by the terminal session' stays for a session with no report and for a bridge older than 1.27.14. Freshness: a local switch shows within one heartbeat (5 s), because the engine's `permission_mode_changed` record reports Shift+Tab as it happens; a remote one shows at the next status read (a watch opening, a model change, or a main-loop step-end at most every 10 s), so an idle remote session's switch waits for its next turn, recorded as a tracker row. 152. **2026-10-30, the architect, under O3, at the landing of PR #369 (merge 67f4baff4): every main-process, host and preload bundle is emitted as Latin-1 text, so V8 holds its source at a byte a character, about 6 MB off the daemon shape's idle footprint.** O3-D's executor traced the rise of `mawsd`'s idle reading on M5's mini run (33,457 KiB, against M2's 30,849 to 31,537 and `MAWSD_HEADROOM_KIB` 33,048) mostly to the scene (compiled code and zod's parsers left warm by an app's turn, about 1 to 1.5 MB, the object diff after release clean) and about 0.3 to 0.4 MB to code, and found a larger lever: Node keeps every loaded file's source in the heap, and one character above U+00FF anywhere in a file makes V8 hold the whole file at two bytes a character. `scripts/latin1-bundles.ts` is an output plugin on main's and the preloads' builds (the `?modulePath` hosts inherit it): each chunk is re-printed by esbuild with `charset: 'ascii'`, acorn places any leftover (comments and regular expression literals take `\uXXXX`; anything else, a tagged template's raw text among them, fails the build), the build fails if any emitted `.js` file still holds such a character, and turning source maps on is refused until they are chained. Measured on the MacBook by V8 heap after a forced GC: the engine host idle 4.24 MiB less, `mawsd` released 1.55 MiB less on O3-D's tip. An AST comparison of master's and the head's builds found 249 files byte-identical and 63 re-printed with the same tree, none different; functions shipped as source (`executeJavaScript`, the search worker) are byte-identical. 157. **2026-10-30, the architect, at the landing of PR #374 (merge b3fef978d): conversation search indexes a growing transcript from where it stopped, so an append no longer re-reads the whole file.** The long-transcript diagnosis (the owner's report that old sessions would not open) found the idle CPU of a large store in `fs.sessionText`, which re-read every transcript from byte 0 on each size change, 2.1 to 3.5 s of CPU per pass on the largest. The index now stores a checkpoint with each text (the byte offset after the last consumed line, the inode, digests of the first and last 4 KiB before the offset), and the host resumes there when the inode, the size and both digests agree, otherwise reading from byte 0; a line counts only once complete, a pass cut short clears the checkpoint, and a prefix rewritten during a resumed pass fails it into a full one. A transcript already indexed is re-indexed at most once per 30 s (`SEARCH_REINDEX_DEBOUNCE_MS`), the first index after a start at once. Measured on a 383 MB synthetic transcript on the loaded MacBook: about 1.0 s of CPU per append before, about 1 ms after (about 21 KB read). This adds index migration 15 (`session_text_state.resume`) and takes `WIRE_MINOR` 8; the long-transcript window (its own PR, still in review) takes the next. Gates: the head's runs 41130970504 and 41133629428, then the merge with master after #376 in run 41135433376 (class 1a, unit:darwin, which the migration and the fs schema reach, and the nine e2e groups the planner named plus e2e:desk-web), and the final merge after #261 in run 41138230305 (class 1a, e2e:desk-web, e2e:desk-web-hub), no flaky line in any. reviewer-medium's two findings: a prefix rewritten during a resumed pass, fixed with a test; a same-length rewrite in the file's middle, which the cheap check cannot see and the engine never makes, a tracker row. 158. **2026-10-30, the architect, under O10, at the landing of PR #378 (merge e56f3f047): an endpoint that holds an event whose signer it does not know asks the hub for the org state, so a newly certified machine's first events no longer wait for the 10-minute read.** The finding came from the session sminos-message-delivery-audit, passed on by the owner and verified on master by the desk track: storing a certificate moves no org-state version, the hub sends `state` only on a new version, so an endpoint learned of a new machine's certificate only at its `SYNC_EVERY_MS` read or at an RL1 welcome, and `dc-linux171-1e68`'s first say after its cutover waited about 2 min on the MacBook. RL10's relay now asks for a read when it holds an event for an unknown signer or key, or for a certificate lapsed at the event's `created`: at most once per key per 60 s, coalesced, per key so that a second new machine inside the window is not starved (the hub's own re-read is one global 1 s window). Retirement stays bounded at the 10-minute read by design (Decision Logs 23 and 24: retirement is not revocation, and remote-architecture adds that a retired key is one destroyed or never at risk, so nothing signs with it during the window; such events never become a person's words either), a tracker row. Revocation: the architect's two tests pass, on master too (a `state` notice adopted with one read and no tick; a revoked key's events refused at once after it). The one path that still waited, a notice whose read fails, the architect and remote-architecture (its message 44) ruled endpoint-side: `#syncTo` retries on the link's backoff toward the highest version announced until the head reaches it, the tick the backstop. A hub resend was rejected (an acknowledgement on the relay and an owner's deploy for the same bound), and so was refusing a carrier's events while behind an announced version: notices are unsigned, so a buggy or hostile hub could stall every event on a machine by announcing a version it never serves, and the guarantee against a hostile hub comes from direct delivery. The lag is then the hub's read unavailability, the right bound for a path that carries availability with an honest hub, and until O8's RL4 delivery with an acknowledgement (the remote-layer spec's revocation of record), `admin remove-device`'s 'within 10 minutes at worst' reads 'within the retry's backoff'. It is built in its own PR, stacked on this one. 161. **2026-10-30, the architect, under O2 and O14, at the landing of PR #381 (merge 3e94bde9f): a `mawsd` whose bundle was replaced under it launches nothing from the new bundle; a unit daemon restarts itself, and a private one refuses with the reason until the next app restarts it.** At O3-D's upgrade of both Macs (the MacBook at 20:53Z, the mini at 23:08Z, reported by the desk track) the old `mawsd`, running on across the app's quit and swap, launched each host three times from its own build's content-hashed entry chunk, which the swapped `app.asar` no longer held (`Cannot find module` on `out/main/index-<hash>.js` in both hosts' logs), and marked both hosts crashed until the new app's build check restarted it. With nothing live that cost about 3 s; a swap followed by any other client first (the sminos bridge, a remote request) would have left both hosts crashed indefinitely. Before each host spawn `mawsd` now reads its entry again (`currentBuildIdOf`, `src/engine-host/supervisor-files.ts`) and compares it with the build id it computed at start. On a mismatch the detached transport's new `beforeSpawn` ends the attempt as `notLaunched`, counting no exit: a `--unit` daemon stops with exit 75, so its unit restarts it from the new bundle and it adopts the running hosts; a `--private` daemon stays up, serves what runs, fails held requests with the reason and parks the host, so the next request checks again. Electron holds each archive it opened for the life of the process, so an ordinary read, even `existsSync`, still answers from the old archive after a swap; the check therefore parses the archive now at that path, with `process.noAsar` set inside one synchronous call (about 2 ms on a 100 MB archive). The same hold makes `mawsd`'s in-process deferred loads (the spine's services chunk, the endpoint chunk) read the old build, shown by a probe on the pinned Electron; from a checkout's `out/main` those loads go through the same check. Linux releases are immutable and the entry is resolved through `current`'s real path, so a flip never reads as a swap. No wire change. Proof: unit tests of both daemon kinds and a process test that swaps a built `out/main` under a running `mawsd`, each failing without its fix (the process scene then shows the original three exits). Gates: runs 41145053613 and 41145178351 (class 1a; e2e:supervisor-daemon, daemon-unit, machines, supervisor, default) and 41146823343 on the head, and run 41147865133 on the merge with master after #377 (`--gates all`), no flaky line; reviewer-medium's one finding, a private daemon left crashed with a stale refusal when the incompatible engine host it waited on exited after the swap, fixed with a test. The field check is the next upgrade of either Mac: no three-exit line in `mawsd.log`.
162. **2026-10-30, the architect, under O10, at the landing of PR #382 (merge 2d88524f1): an endpoint whose read fails after a `state` notice retries until its head reaches the announced version, so a revocation no longer waits for the 10-minute read.** Built as the architect and remote-architecture ruled at #378 (Decision Log 158): `#syncTo` keeps the highest version announced, and a chase retries through the link's `RecordRetries` under the key `sync` (about 1 s first, about 7 s after three failures) until the head reaches it, settling on any adoption that reaches it, on stop or at the link's end; the next welcome reads anyway, and the 10-minute read stays the backstop. Until O8's RL4 delivery with an acknowledgement, this is `admin remove-device`'s path, and its 'within 10 minutes at worst' reads 'within the retry's backoff'. A fail-first test: a revocation notice whose two reads both fail is adopted at the first retry, and the revoked key's event is refused at once. Gates: run 41146987836 on the head (static, unit, e2e:desk-web, e2e:desk-web-hub) and run 41148010890 on the merge with master after #377 and #381 (the same set with probes), no flaky line; reviewer-medium twice, its one finding (an adoption that reaches the version now settles the chase) fixed.
163. **2026-10-31, the architect, as P5's writer under O3 (its acceptance 5, raised by O3's recomposition audit): OX1's exception covers the larger residual O3-D built and measured, and the contract now bounds the residual by the landing that moves the state instead of limiting it to writes made by hand.** Decision Log 101 ruled on what a refused pre-O3-D app can write by hand beside a post-D `mawsd`. O3-D found two more ways such an app shares the profile. (1) Its `daemon.restart` is honoured (O3-D's D29): O2's build check makes it replace or re-install its own daemon, the post-D `mawsd` yields to it within seconds, and the old app then runs its spine alone, one writer; refusing it would leave that app with no engine host and no daemon of its own. (2) An app from before O3-A takes no `spine.lock`, so its main spine runs from its launch until `mawsd` yields on Electron's `SingletonLock` signal: in that window it writes the spine's non-index files (overlaps of 356 and 628 ms measured, bounded by `REFUSAL_TO_RELEASE_MS`, 5 s from the refusal), never `index.sqlite`, which it opens read-only at version 14 against its 13, and the Needs-you items raised while it was away never reach it (O3 Decision Log 19(4)). Ruled acceptable: only a downgrade or an old app left installed meets either case, the supported path is upgrade, both Macs already run post-D builds, and a shipped build cannot be changed to take the lock. O3's acceptance 5 is met with this residual, and OX1's sentence and O3 §2 now say so. Rejected: refusing `daemon.*` from a refused app, which strands it; holding the yield until the old app shows it has stopped writing, which a pre-A build has no way to signal.

Two conditions bind it: an app that next connects sees the connector signed in through `connectors.signInChanged`, never a stuck connecting state, and the loopback redirect listener lives no longer than the flow's deadline. O5 M3 (advisory, for every Electron root): under Playwright's loader an exception thrown before `ready` shows only as an `electron.launch` timeout with nothing on stderr, because Electron's modal error dialog holds Chromium's start; it cost O5 two gate rounds (`require.main` is unset under the loader, so a relative read threw). The render host now writes its failure with `writeSync` and exits 70, which a windowless process under launchd needs anyway; any other root started without a window takes the same rule. PR #405: the `cli-prompt` ^C flake, nine sightings across children that never touched `src/mawsd/`, was a race in the product: `hiddenPrompt` wrote the question before `setRawMode(true)`, so input arriving the moment the prompt showed met a cooked terminal, echoed and with ^C turned into SIGINT. A person typing could not hit a window of two back-to-back calls, but a program answering the prompt (a script, an agent in a pty) could get the enrollment code echoed into the terminal and its scrollback. Raw mode now comes first, as getpass(3) orders it; the ^C case checks the terminal is raw at the prompt, and the test helper reports the output it actually timed out on. Reproduced by a 300 ms pause between the two calls (three of four tests failed) and passing in the new order; 30 of 30 local runs under load. Gates: class 1a (41215002392); reviewer-medium found nothing material. The tracker row of 2026-10-28 keeps only its two remaining items. The wave 2 ledger gains O5 M3's names (the render client capability, `render.answer`'s `ipcError`, the render library's and host's exports, the moved design-system files, `e2e:render-host`) and O4 M2's e2e seam `MAWS_E2E_DELIVERY_HOLD`.
172. **2026-10-31, the architect, after O3: the renderer's other first reads survive an update's `mawsd` restart, and a snapshot not yet loaded never trims the restored layout (PR #406, merge 155eb0007).** Decision Log 170 found that the same restart window reaches the reads besides the launch mode. One helper (`src/renderer/src/ipc/until-answered.ts`, which #403's launch-mode ask now uses) asks a read again when main reports the local machine's `host.status`, and at once if one arrived while the read was out; a rejected call counts as unanswered. It now serves the local snapshot in `events.ts` (`session.list`, `session.projects.list`, `session.digests`, `attention.list`), `session.intent.list`, `session.deletions.list` and the local `laneA.list`, which buffers its change events again while a retry is out. The invariant: the local machine counts as loaded only once its records and the tab retention's intents are in, and until then its tabs are kept as a connecting remote machine's already are, so `repairMain` drops nothing and nothing trimmed is saved; `settleMachine` then drops what is really gone. A failed `session.intent.list` is now unknown, not empty, and a restored tab's empty state no longer closes it as gone while its machine is unloaded (the review's one finding, which also protects a connecting remote machine's tabs). Normal startup is unchanged: hydration waits for the first answers, not for success. Left as is: `attention.pendingNavigate` is main's and never crosses the restart; another machine's failed snapshot is retaken at its next link generation, since `host.status` speaks only for the local machine; and whether the sidebar's skeleton waits for the local machine to load is a UX call, registered as board #407 (deferred). Gates: all on the hosted lane (41211435139), its tree the PR head's, no flaky test, the cross-build scene (update, mawsd restart) now also writing a session tab into the profile's layout and reading it back whole; reviewer-medium's one P2 fixed and re-reviewed clean. It landed without a re-gate on the merge: master had moved by #404 (gate-plan) and #405 (the enrollment prompt), neither on its path. The wave 2 ledger gains O7 M2's history names (`remote-history.ts`, `remote.historyTooLarge`).
173. **2026-10-31, the architect, for O3-D's spine and O16's handover: a spine load caused only by the engine host's events no longer wakes an engine host that no one wants (PR #409, merge d3b3ea2c8), and a machine that another paired app is watching stays loaded.** O16 M2b found the race. After the engine host was asked to exit when idle, its link closed and the index source's `fs.bind` failed, but `sync()` still sent `fs.state` while the supervisor showed the host restarting. The supervisor held that request and, at the deliberate close, launched a successor, so a host that was parking came back. The fix: each spine load is marked wanted or not from its trigger. A command's or the endpoint's `ensure` makes it wanted, and so does a later need on a load the host's events started (`src/mawsd/spine-seam.ts`). Every spine request to the engine host passes through `SpineHostsAdapter.dispatch` (`src/mawsd/spine-hosts.ts`). For a load that is not wanted, while the host is not ready, it refuses the request with `host_unavailable` and never hands it to the supervisor. Nothing is lost: every part that talks to the engine host repeats its requests at each welcome. At the host's ready that means `engine.configure`, `engine.policy.set`, `host.configure` and `artifacts.setDefaults`; at its streaming, the index sync; at each barrier, the resync. Two alternatives were rejected. Skipping requests to a parking host fails because the spine cannot see that exit when idle is armed, and a ready but armed host may still serve live rows. Never sending anything from an event-caused load fails because a host welcomed while the spine was cold would never be configured. O16's handover must keep this invariant: a draining or exiting host, or a successor not yet welcomed, counts as not ready, and the successor must raise the link's ready and streaming transitions. Gates: static, unit, probes, `e2e:supervisor-daemon`, `e2e:supervisor`, `e2e:daemon-unit` and `e2e:settings` passed in run 41220396581, whose tree is the fix commit's, with no flaky test; `unit:darwin` is not reached. Master had moved only by #408, which changes CI only. Reviewer-medium found one real gap, and it predates this PR. On the daemon shape, a command whose handler waits for the session service to boot never answers while the spine is cold and the engine host parked, because only an app attaching or the discovery wake starts a parked host. That is P5's core scenario: a paired app opening a session on a machine whose own app is quit. So it goes to a follow-up PR on master now, not to the tracker row #409 added. The desk track's item 5 found the MacBook's spine still loaded three hours after its app quit at 14:02:42Z, with no quiet entry when the app left. The mini's app, paired minutes earlier, kept an endpoint channel open to the MacBook. Every open channel holds `mawsd` (`hold('channel')` in `src/endpoint/channels.ts`); the daemon's quiet requires no holds, and the spine is released only when the daemon is quiet. The engine host's park clock looks only at apps and interest, so the host parked as usual. Ruling: a machine that another paired app is watching stays loaded, so that app's reads answer without first waiting about 2.5 s for the spine to load. Item 5's cold reading will be taken while no other paired app has a channel open. The tech-debt tracker gains a row for `e2e:discover`, whose catalog-backed assertions use the default 5 s timeout; it missed twice today, and each time the rerun passed.
174. **2026-10-31, the architect, for O3-D's spine and every child that touches `mawsd`: a command that waits on the engine host now wakes a parked one (PR #411, merge 4118fa60a), and O4 M2 is accepted at its first stop.** #411 treats the wait itself as the need. Every session-service command waits in `SessionService.ready()`. While the boot has not finished waiting for the host's first streaming, that wait calls `host.need()`, which the spine root passes to its hosts. The adapter forwards the need only for a wanted load, so a load caused by an event still wakes nothing (Decision Log 173's rule). It keeps the need until the load streams, and tells it again if the link leaves ready first. That re-telling fixes reviewer-medium's one finding: a need told while the barrier resynced left the boot stranded when the host had been asked to exit when idle. `mawsd`'s `needEngine` does two things. It restarts the park clock from the need; without that, the woken host is asked to park the moment it is ready, and the next idle cycle's command never answers. It also sends `engine.ping` to a host that is not streaming, which the supervisor holds and launches a parked host for. A need that arrives during a discovery wake leaves that wake's park to the clock. Two alternatives were rejected. A list of the commands that need the host (`beforeSpineCommand`'s pattern) would have to be kept in step with the handlers. `hosts.wake` acts only on a host already parked, not on one still closing. Tests that fail on master: a daemon-in-process test in which a `machine.sock` `session.intent.list` and the endpoint's `session.create` and `session.acquire` answer within the discovery wake's 8 s bound across two idle cycles, each cycle ending parked and cold, while `attention.list` wakes nothing; four seam cases; two clock cases; and `e2e:supervisor-daemon`'s case (n), in which a parked host is woken over `machine.sock` and the private daemon then exits by itself. Gates: static, unit, probes, `e2e:supervisor-daemon`, `e2e:supervisor`, `e2e:daemon-unit` and `e2e:settings` passed in run 41225519070, on the PR head's tree, with no flaky test; `unit:darwin` is not reached. #411 removes the tracker row #409 added; the merge's conflict with Decision Log 173's new tracker row was resolved by hand. O16's handover must keep three rules: a request to a parked or closing host is still held and launches a host, the old host leaving ready reaches the spine's link, and a need restarts the park clock. O4 M2 (code head a1d727f76, wire 13 against master's 12) is accepted at stop (1). Its gates: class 1a (41226571943); cross-build against the 3.12 peer (41225432077); `--gates all` on the hosted lane (41215859564, 48 jobs green, its three reds fixed and rerun green); `e2e:discover` on the mini (41222546081); `e2e:dashboard-live-data` (41223857261); and `e2e:delivery` with `e2e:connectors` (41226572706). O4 also found that `e2e:dashboard-live-data`'s long case has been red on master since UX2-L removed the header's More actions button. Nobody saw it because this opt-in spec runs in no gate job. It is fixed on O4's branch and lands with it. O4 goes on to M3 after merging master; the load its dataset refresh triggers must be wanted under Decision Log 173's rule. `process-yield.test`'s design-system seed flake reached its fourth sighting (O4's class 1a run 41225432077), and a worker is fixing the test on master. The wave 2 ledger gains #409's and #411's spine names, O4 M2's delivery log line and the shared file `e2e/support/cross-build-samples.ts`.

176. **2026-10-31, the architect, for O3-D's yield test: the design-system seed flake in `src/mawsd/process-yield.test.ts` is explained and fixed in the test (PR #415, merge 999739301).** The engine host seeds its built-in design systems at its first start over an empty store. The test now loads the spine with `attention.list`, which takes the same load path (the lock, the index migration, the parts started) and wakes no engine host; it asserts after the release that the daemon log has no `spawned a supervisor` line, and keeps the whole-index comparison, whose failure message now carries the daemon log. Under load the old test failed 1 in 160 and the new one passed 200 of 200; a write injected into the yield's stop fails it 3 of 3. Two readings recorded, neither changed here. O3-D's D53 (a), that a yield's writes are only its stores' close, holds for an idle root: a busy root's release waits for it, and the library sync, outside `settling` by design, can still apply host answers meanwhile. And the discovery wake parks the engine host while the library's first sync may still be in flight, so after a cold `session.list` the library can stay unreconciled (seeded design systems missing, stale rows listed) until the next host connection; that is a tracker row and an input to O4's library reads. Waiting for the park was rejected: nothing in the e2e shows it except the daemon's status read, whose supervisor status wakes the host itself. Found by reading the code, not observed: an idle exit whose process outlives its socket by more than one pid poll reads as a quiet loss, and the host is relaunched after the restart backoff instead of parked; a tracker row, and an input to O16. 182. **2026-10-31, the architect, at the fix for the engine host's idle CPU after the MacBook's swap (PR #419, merge 2d24e589b).** From 20:35Z to about 21:00Z the MacBook's engine host ran at 225 to 290% CPU with no session working. The desk track traced it to `src/engine-host/fs/service.ts`: every change to an agent sidecar file re-ran a full `SubagentReader.list` walk of that session's subagents, for every bound session, and main's `fs.scan` binds them all. One session had 4,233 subagent files and a workflow writing about 75 a minute, and one walk of that tree costs about 0.6 to 0.8 s of CPU. The fix: `SubagentReader.update` brings a listing up to date from only the changed files and answers null (a full walk) for anything it cannot place; one pass at a time per session, change-driven passes at most every 100 ms after the first and 5 s after a failed one; and change-driven listing only for sessions something reads (a follow, or the clients' `host.interest`, which mawsd's interest union already carries for the app and for the endpoint's remote viewers). Every follower poke waits until the agent changes reported so far are read, because the engine writes a child's meta before the parent's `Agent` result. A committed bench (`subagents-bench.test.ts`, run only with `MAWS_BENCH=1`) reproduces the incident on 4,040 files: before, 220 walks and 271% CPU at 10 writes a second, and 441 walks, 325% and a 23.6 s backlog at 40; after, no walks and 1 to 2%. Reviewer-medium ran three rounds: four P2s in the follow ordering, all fixed with tests that fail on the earlier versions, then none. Gates: the worker's run on the head's tree (41251802912: static, unit, `unit:darwin`, probes and eight e2e groups, no flaky test), then the architect's `--gates all` on the hosted lane on the merge commit, since an engine host change reaches every group through the build: run 41253276757. Left, as tracker rows the PR adds: the 30 s reconcile tick still walks a session held open (about 0.7 s CPU for 4,000 files, about 2% of a core), and a reconcile or rescan poke inside the watcher's 50 ms debounce of a child's meta write can still fold the parent's result first, a window older than this change. A client that never reports `host.interest` no longer hears `fs.subagentsChanged`; every current app does. The fix reaches the owner's Macs at their next build swap.

184. **2026-11-01, the architect, O12 gains M11, the spawner as owner (the desk track's PR #423, merge 8ad896717, docs; static 41266345501 on a tree equal to the merge's).** O7 M4d found that a lane B session spawned from machine A cannot be opened until B's sminos lists it, because B's endpoint ranks a lane B session only from its sminos record (`sessionRank`). O7 waits main-side, up to 60 s, for a session it spawned itself, saying it is waiting for the machine to list it rather than that the machine cannot place it. The end state is O12's: B's endpoint ranks `launchedBy.member` as owner, and the sminos record carries that member as owner from the session's first SessionStart. Routing ruled: the launch environment's `MAWS_LAUNCHED_BY` and its proof file ride in M11's own PR after O7 M4, not in O7 or O16, so the producer and consumer of that contract land together under one owner; O7 M4 pins `launchedBy` written once, by `session.spawn`'s handler alone, since it is now a trust input. Two adversarial review rounds changed the agreed shape in three places: the bridge keys on a durable handed-off mark (`handed-off.json` in the endpoint's state directory; once a record has been seen for a session, a missing record fails closed), /clear's successor takes its predecessor's record by appSessionId (an opId can recur after `KEPT_S`), and the proof is an envelope of member, device, opId and action.
185. **2026-11-01, the architect, O5 landing 1's measured series (on its merge commit 34946761a, CI-opt, the mini) met every bound, so landing 2 needs no fix forward.** `e2e:daemon-idle`: 92,163, 91,587 and 92,947 KiB (41260374968, 41262619666, 41263684431), median 92,163 against 99,145, one void (41261509395, diffuse load). `e2e:daemon-idle-render`: 94,643, 95,251 and 94,195 KiB (41264877782, 41266094952, 41267184990), median 94,643, no voids, and no render host in any run's process set, so the host's quiet exit holds. `e2e:local-latency-daemon` (41268381349): delta-to-frame p50 24.6 and p95 37.4 ms, round trip p50 1.2 and p95 1.6 ms. After a render `mawsd` keeps 2 to 3 MB more than at plain idle (34,145 to 35,265 against 32,017 to 32,481 KiB). With O4's roughly 1 MB in `mawsd`, the shared idle headroom after a render is about 2 to 3 per cent before O16 and O7, so O5 is asked to find what `mawsd` retains after the host exits and release it where cheap, or record the figure. U3's macOS half (dec050198) measured nothing on its first run: the spike's host imported its Linux peer, which reads `/proc/stat` at module load, so it threw on darwin; O5 fixes it and CI-opt reruns it inside the same hold.
186. **2026-11-01, the architect, O5's render-host scene fixed under the packaged app (PR #426, merge c2ddf0a4c), M7's verdict, and the browser step cut from O5 to a new child, O17.** `e2e/render-host.spec.ts`'s main-stderr scene (then line 144, now 168) had failed every time under the packaged binary since landing 1 (#418): its listener attached after `_electron.launch` resolved, and the packaged binary, whose ready no loader holds, wrote its line before then. It reached master unseen because no landing gate built the packaged app, which the default set now does where it takes a hosted Mac (gate-offload Decision Log 158, PR #425). O5's fix runs the binary under a wrapper that tees main's stderr to a file from its spawn. Gates: static and packaged on the head (41269375486, the scene passed on packaged 2/2), and `e2e:render-host` on hosted on the merge tree (41271374148, 2 passed) for the checkout-Electron path the wrapper also changed. M7 (U3, the agent's browser for a remote session) promotes in offscreen tab views. Three runs on the mini (CI-opt; a3a0d7c87, then 6b3fab2df) found that hidden windows paint nothing and hidden-unthrottled captures nothing on Linux, while offscreen works on both, at about 110 ms click to paint on macOS with 100 ms page timers near 6 Hz, which neither a power-save blocker (so not App Nap) nor turning background throttling off lifts. The viewer's collapse to 0 fps came from sharing the tab's one CDP screencast with the agent, whose stopScreencast ended it for both; the frame subscription held its own rate through the agent's screenshot, detach and re-attach. M7's seven conditions bind the browser step. Landing 2 is M5 to M7, not M5 to M8, because O7 M5 reads `session.capabilities`, which reaches master only with landing 2. The browser step's re-plan came to about 4,400 lines against M7's own bar of about 3,000, and its adversarial review found contracts with O16 (an engine-host browser-work fence read through `holdsWork`), with O7 and O10 (`view` for frames, a session-bound `converse` act for input) and with cua's owner (cua #242, its peer policy on Linux), and a P1 (a retained headless tab with no recovery-safe close). So it is cut to a new P5 child, O17, with a local stage and a remote stage; its section follows from O5's proposal, and O5 closes after landing 2 and its measured series. O5's after-render retention in `mawsd` (+2.6 MiB) is module code, mostly undici and web streams shared with any later `fetch` user (O10's enrolled endpoint already is one), with nothing releasable.
187. **2026-11-01, the architect, O17 is cut from O5: the agent's browser with no app, and its remote view (O5's `[cut]` proposal; Decision Log 186 named the cut).** The section is O5's proposal folded with the architect's six rulings on the M8 re-plan. The cold broker in `mawsd` answers cua's hello and empty inventory, so only the first real browser primitive starts the render host: cua connects eagerly and retries every 5 s, so a connection trigger would restart Chromium at every quiet period. One bound, quiescent listener per enrolled session with a kept session and no app is allowed, its cost measured; a cold daemon start has none. `host.browserWork` is O17's on both sides, read through `holdsWork` in `src/engine-host/budget.ts`, which O16 found to be the one chokepoint of the budget, idle and verified-empty releases; every adopted session reads `unknown` until `mawsd`'s first snapshot applies, nothing enters O16's checkpoint or journal, a pre-O17 app's tabs keep today's behaviour as a recorded gap, and an engine host without the capability gets no headless browser. Linux reads unavailable with its reason until cua #242 (cua's peer policy on Linux, after cua #171's M3) and a two-repo scene pass. Remote input is a session-bound `converse` act, revocable on its own and never implied by `view`; O7 M5 and O10 accept or tighten it before the remote stage. The local stage lands after O5's landing 2 and O16, the remote stage with or after O7 M5. 188. **2026-11-01, the architect, at O7 M4's landing (diff, files, uploads and spawn on another machine, and a remote session's history in pages the channel carries; PR #427, merge da1cc2563).** `WIRE_MINOR` is 15, master's 14 plus one, and `RL2_PROTOCOL` is 1.2 with the capabilities `rl2.call-session`, `rl2.feeds` and `rl2.events-budget`; O5's landing 2, O16 and O7's next landing each re-take master's minor plus one at their landing. Two changes reach beyond M4's own rows. First, lane B placement no longer tells a caller below the floor whether a session exists. When the engine host is up but its live row names another engine session than the index's, a caller below the floor on the recorded one hears `grade`, not `unavailable`. When the host is away, every lane B id answers `unavailable` before any rank check. This narrows O10's entry of 2026-10-31 (#414) to a caller with rank on the recorded session. The narrowing is O7's, recorded here: O10 owes nothing, and there is no fix in the shared path, whatever #427's body says. O12 M11, which edits that path, keeps this order and corrects its own plan text. Second, `sessions.launched_by` is added by `ADDED_COLUMNS`, not by a numbered migration. Two index files at version 15 may therefore differ by that nullable column, and the previous build keeps its index on a downgrade. A table rebuild must carry its table's added columns, which a test guards. Residue routed: ranking `launchedBy.member` as owner, and the endpoint ledger's `orphanAtOpen`, go to O12 M11 (`launchedBy` is now write-once, by `session.spawn`'s handler alone). `host.op`'s orphan claims go to O16 M3c, which opens the engine host's ledger with the same option. The reload that loses a draft handed to a new remote session is board #429, a deferred UX ticket. The checkout rule's realpath check-then-use window is a tracker row. O7 goes on with M3, then M6, under the unlaned placement; M5 waits for O5's landing 2 and its `session.capabilities`. The wave 2 ledger gains O7 M4's names and its two shared files.
189. **2026-11-01, the architect, O17's execution document landed (PR #430, merge ab0c95788, docs; static 41292356667 on its head, tree 35e24321), and the O17 section is corrected from its design round.** The document cites this one at pin 639261356. It has ten milestones: the local stage, M1 to M8, as one landing, of which only M7 waits for O16's landing; then the remote stage, M9 and M10. Its decisions are D1 to D20, each with the alternative it rejected. A fable spec review and the adversarial reviewer on buildability found 2 P1 and 8 P2 between them, all adopted after checking against the code, and the re-check was clean. The estimate is about 6,100 lines (local 4,475, remote 1,590, range about 4,900 to 7,300), against the section's 4,400. The review's fixes, parts the old estimate left implicit, and the idle exception make up the difference; it is accepted as an estimate, not a bar. Required: no, by the owner's answer of 2026-11-01. O15 records any stage not landed as carried. The architect's rulings on its route list. R5: only headless tabs are fenced. An app's agent tab keeps today's release and stays in the panel as the person's tab, because fencing it would hold every laptop session whose agent left a tab open past the owner's 10-minute release. The `machine.browserEngine` welcome capability went with it. R2: `machine.browserControl {appSessionId, epoch}` is graded `approve`, not `converse`, because a person's input into the agent's tab bypasses the tool-permission prompt the agent's own call may meet. It is bound to the session and its epoch, revocable on its own and never implied by `view`. A `browser.inventory` feed at `view` replaces the `machine.browserInventory` act. O10 accepted both, with four pins for M9's tests: a non-owner steps up per grant and per epoch, web-cards devices are refused, the act stays idempotent under the endpoint ledger's `orphanAtOpen`, and input is re-authorized from the Authorizer's record read. R1: O7 M5 owns the session-bound read open, which its own `http` and `document` streams also need, and reserves the `browser.screencast` kind and `rl2.browser-screencast`. O17 M9 builds on it after O7 M5, with no joint landing; O7's plan had held no such reservation. R4: O16 confirmed that the engine host keeps the link's last snapshot per process, so a session adopted after the first snapshot takes its verdict. Nothing enters O16's checkpoint, mawsd sends the snapshot first on every fresh link, a successor's included, and the hold lives in `holdsWork` alone. R6: cua's Linux contract is cua #242, and the broker's protocol behaviours are cua #251's contract tests, each naming O17. The idle exception (D20): the line falls between the budget release, which can take a session one quiet sample after its turn and is fenced, and the idle release. The idle release comes only after the machine role's whole idle time (10 minutes on a laptop, 24 hours on a server) with no turn, interest or other work. At its due time a row held only by the browser hold waits with no timer and emits `host.browserIdle`, `mawsd` drops the session's headless tabs, and the row is reaped once the hold clears. O16's verified-empty release is a step of the reap, not a third path. M7 now also edits O16's `idle-release.ts` and `process-table.ts`, which O16 sees at its landing. Accepted by design (D12): P5 §2's rule that restarting `mawsd` never ends a session holds for the session but not its headless tabs. The render host is `mawsd`'s child and exits with its link. The section changes: purpose's estimate, acceptance 2's counter (O5 M5's `PENDING_BUDGET` extended, not a second constant), acceptance 4 (headless only, the idle exception, late adoptions, an app's tabs by rule), acceptance 9's stale-epoch reading, acceptance 10's grant, the edges and OX9 (the engines' commands in a new uniform-`internal` namespace `browserEngine`, the feed, `approve`, `browser.remote.*`), and the fence seam. The wave 2 ledger gains O17's names. O17's execution starts after O5's landing 2.
190. **2026-11-01, the architect, at O12 M11's landing on the MAWS side (the spawner as owner; the desk track's PR #431, merge 15c8f02e5).** `WIRE_MINOR` is 16 (`session.launch`'s `spec.launchedBy`), master's 15 plus one; O5's landing 2, O16 and O7's next landing each re-take master's minor plus one. sminos's half landed first (doperpowers #450, a16a0807d, sminos 1.28.0). A lane B session that a member spawns on another machine through `session.spawn` is now that member's from its first second. The spine writes a 0600 proof file, `<userData>/sessions/launched-by/<appSessionId>.json`, before each launch that carries `launchedBy`, and `MAWS_LAUNCHED_BY` names it. Until a sminos record has been seen for the session, B's endpoint answers its owners as the spawner alone, private. The handed-off mark (`handed-off.json`) makes a missing record fail closed with `grade` once a record has been seen, across a restart, a `/clear` and a `gone`. The endpoint's op ledger opens with `orphanAtOpen`. That closes the two items Decision Log 188 routed to M11, and keeps that entry's order for an index lag. sminos's binding file is `launched/<sha256(appSessionId)>.jsonl`, not the name O12's Interfaces §H gave; O12's Decision Log entry "M11's MAWS half as built" records that correction with the plan's others. The step now pins M11's end state: the pane opens B's session with nothing listed, never says it cannot place it, and shows no waiting line. `--gates all` on that head, d10985d01 (41299237023): 55 green, including `e2e:remote`, `e2e:cross-build`, `e2e:desk-web` and `packaged`, with no flaky pass. The one red was unit 2/4's `src/engine-host/documents/search.test.ts:300`, outside M11's reach; it passed on 4f908f8bd and on `--gates unit` on the same head (41303512172), and is a new tracker row. Reviews: reviewer-high in two rounds, every code finding fixed, then reviewer-medium on the merged branch, whose P1 folded into residue (b). Residue: (a) an unwritable state directory loses the sight across a restart, accepted in O12's Decision Log. (b) A record the join cannot place under its app session holds the bridge on the new engine session until that session's record arrives; this is a tracker row due before O8's record actions move owners, and an edge in O8's section. (c) Unpruned engine-session marks and (d) a dev build's Diagnostics resume without the spawner are tracker rows. O7 drops its main-side wait of up to 60 s for the listing at its next merge of master.

It ended as the installer's own open file description, locked by `flock 3`, so the lock is owned rather than checked (O5's §J), and a Linux test pins that no child but flock inherits it. reviewer-high on the three parts of the range (49208407e..e29ef74ac, the master merge 18a20684d, and the install lock's 3cca1fe67..e7698f43b) found nothing material, so no second panel ran.
